# 210: Chapter 211: Absolutely Despicable!

"Wait… was that Will just now?"

As he hurried down the corridor, Proton suddenly froze mid-step. He could have sworn he just heard Will's voice—was he yelling… Karen's name?

"I heard it too. Looks like they ran into Dylan," Lt. Surge said gravely.

"In that case, there's no point in hiding anymore. Dylan is already inside—we should use this chance to head for the exit."

Archer spoke in a serious tone. As much as he hated to admit it, the two new executives were beyond saving right now.

If an opportunity arose in the future, they could think about rescuing them then.

"Agreed!"

Proton and Lt. Surge immediately nodded, the three of them dashing toward the exit.

"At least we have a lot of executives present this time. That lowers our chances of running into Dylan. Once we escape safely, I'll make sure to visit them often."

Proton exhaled, trying to lift the heavy mood.

"Proton, for the love of Arceus, please stop jinxing us," Archer groaned, his temple twitching.

By now, he was convinced that Proton was cursed—every time he opened his mouth, disaster struck.

"Archer! We're companions, aren't we? How could you say that?"

Proton looked deeply hurt.

Jokes aside, Archer was one of his closest allies—they had been through hell together!

"It's not that I don't trust you…"

Archer sighed, realizing his words might've been too harsh. Back when he was at his lowest, Proton had been the one to help him regain his confidence.

Damn it… I'm a terrible friend!

Archer cursed himself internally. Proton is my companion!

But before he could say anything more—

"Oh~ I knew it! Archer, Proton, we really are destined to meet again!"

A familiar, cheerful voice echoed from around the corner.

Archer, Proton, and Lt. Surge froze in place.

Their faces drained of color.

Archer's words caught in his throat, his entire body stiff as he turned to look ahead.

A figure dressed in black slowly emerged, flanked by two young women.

Dylan.

"Dylan…"

Archer's already pale complexion turned deathly white. His expression was that of a man who had just seen a ghost.

Proton and Lt. Surge were equally shaken, their eyes filled with sheer terror.

And then—

Both Archer and Lt. Surge snapped their heads toward Proton, glaring at him with dagger-sharp stares.

You goddamn jinx! You ACTUALLY summoned him here!!

Proton was completely dumbfounded. What the hell is wrong with my mouth?!

"Fate never allows us to miss each other, you see? We always find our way back together. It's destiny."

Dylan's warm, gentle smile remained unchanged as he looked at the three men, his gaze lingering especially long on Archer.

"Archer, are you surprised?"

At those words, Archer stumbled back a step, clutching his chest. His throat felt tight, his breathing rapid.

"Dylan!!" Archer gritted his teeth. Am I surprised? AM I SURPRISED?! Of course I am!!

"Even though we stand on opposite sides, I've always considered you and Proton as close companions."

Dylan's voice was full of sincerity.

"After I send you both to prison for rehabilitation, once you've completed your sentence, I promise I'll personally come pick you up. Then, we'll celebrate together over drinks."

Dylan smiled warmly.

"Though, I can't say for sure if you'll ever finish your sentence."

…Wait… Is he… serious?!

Lt. Surge's eyes nearly popped out of his skull.

How could someone say something so terrifying with such an honest, straight face?!

And the worst part was—Lt. Surge couldn't sense a single trace of insincerity in Dylan's words.

Dylan actually meant it.

What kind of twisted logic is this?!

"I consider you my best companion, so I'm going to send you to prison for hard labor"—HOW does that make sense?!

Lt. Surge was in shock.

"Gee, thanks a lot…"

Archer squeezed those words out through clenched teeth.

"No need to thank me, Archer. You know, I'll never forget the first time we met. You were elegant, charismatic—even in the face of danger, you remained unshaken. It left a deep impression on me."

Dylan placed a hand on his chest, his smile radiating genuine admiration.

"I still remember it vividly."

"I REGRET NOT BLOWING US BOTH UP WHEN I HAD THE CHANCE!!" Archer practically screamed.

Back then, he had been on a ship full of Electrode. Why hadn't he detonated them all and taken Dylan down with him?!

He should have done it.

He should have done it.

"This is exactly why I say you're too extreme, Archer. Besides, I had Mew in my bag back then. Even if you had set off those explosions, I would have been perfectly fine."

Dylan sighed.

Archer felt another sharp pain in his chest. He quickly covered his mouth, struggling to suppress the urge to cough up blood.

But the longer he looked at Dylan's bright, genuine smile, the harder it was to contain himself. His vision darkened—

Thud.

Archer collapsed.

"ARCHER!!"

"Shit! He passed out—what do we do?!"

"We need a medic! No, wait—chest compressions! Tilt his head back—"

Proton and Lt. Surge immediately rushed to their fallen companion's side. Lt. Surge, who had way too much experience in this area, started giving orders.

Dylan, watching the unconscious Archer, fell into deep thought.

"Whoa. Dylan, you're insane! You're the first person I've ever seen knock out a Team Rocket Executive from sheer rage! Respect!"

Leah gave him a thumbs-up, visibly impressed.

Lillie: (—)

Dylan: "…"

After a few minutes of emergency care, Archer slowly regained consciousness—only to be greeted by Lt. Surge's face, looming way too close.

"Wha… what happened…?"

Archer's head was still spinning.

"Archer, you need to take better care of your health. How did you suddenly get anemia?"

Dylan's tone was full of concern.

Archer's mind cleared in an instant.

Right… I PASSED OUT.

"Dylan!!"

Archer shot to his feet, glaring at him.

"Anemia?! My ass! You pissed me off so much I blacked out!!"

"Ah, don't worry, Archer. Once you're in prison, you won't have that problem anymore," Dylan assured him with a smile.

"Oh, and Athena has been waiting for you. You won't be lonely."

"CURSE YOU, DYLAN! YOU'RE TOO ARROGANT!!"

Proton finally snapped. Dylan was way too full of himself!

"Oh?"

Dylan raised an eyebrow, eyes gleaming with interest.

"Sounds like you want to challenge me, Proton? Well, I don't mind. I've missed battling you guys, too."

Dylan waved his hand, and Pecharunt drifted forward, its body glowing faintly with violet energy.

The moment Dylan made a move, Proton immediately retreated, positioning Archer and Lt. Surge in front of him.

"Companions, what do we do now?"

Proton's face twisted in frustration. Do we actually fight him? Because I'm pretty sure we can't win…

Archer and Lt. Surge twitched, giving Proton a look so sharp it could cut steel.

"Don't be afraid, you guys! Take down the evil Dylan! Go, brave Rocket members!!"

Leah cheered them on enthusiastically, clearly enjoying the chaos.

Lillie: (▽｀)

"It looks like there's no way out of this today," Archer muttered, taking a deep breath to steady himself. He straightened his back and pulled out a Poké Ball.

"Dylan! We fight to the death today! Even if our opponent is you, we will never surrender!"

Archer believed in his unyielding spirit. His presence was like that of a warrior preparing for a heroic last stand.

"That's right! Team Rocket never kneels!" Proton passionately echoed his companion's words, hyping him up.

"Dylan, I, Lt. Surge, am no coward!" Lt. Surge grinned ferociously. Since there was no escape, they might as well go all out!

"You guys truly are admirable. But… Pecharunt."

Dylan's eyes gleamed with amusement as he watched the three executives make their final stand. Such commendable determination… Let's make this even more fun.

"Lt. Surge, Proton."

"Huh?"

Just as Lt. Surge and Proton opened their mouths, Pecharunt flicked its mask, sending two small, sticky poison mochis flying straight into their mouths.

"What the hell is this?!"

Lt. Surge and Proton's expressions changed immediately. They desperately tried to spit out the foreign substance, but it had already dissolved.

Then…

"Proton? Lt. Surge?"

Archer stared in shock as the two turned around and faced him.

Wait… Did you guys just defect?! How could you betray me?!

"Are you possessed?!"

Seeing the eerie purple glow in Lt. Surge and Proton's eyes, Archer immediately understood. He was worried—this had to be some trick of that bizarre, ugly Pokémon.

"Tok-Tok-Tok~"

Pecharunt let out a raspy, mischievous laugh, swaying its body slightly.

Lt. Surge and Proton's eyes flickered with an eerie purple light. They raised their arms in unison—

And started dancing.

Left, right, left, right. Up, down, up, down. Left, right, left, right.

Their moves were… surprisingly well-coordinated.

They're… dancing?!

Archer: "…???"

His eye twitched violently as he watched his two companions perform a choreographed dance routine.

"Tiki-Tiki-Tiki~"

As they chanted some incomprehensible rhythm, they simultaneously tossed out Poké Balls.

A Raichu and a Crobat appeared in front of them.

The two Pokémon hesitated, looking at Archer, then back at their trainers in confusion.

What's happening?

But their instincts told them that a battle was imminent, so they focused their attention on Archer.

"Dylan! You're absolutely evil!! You're a Gym Leader!!"

Archer was fuming—he finally understood what was happening.

Dylan had used some underhanded, disgusting technique to control Lt. Surge and Proton, and now he was making them fight him?!

What kind of vile person does this?!

"What kind of righteous person would do something like this?! How can you be so wicked?!"

Dylan's actions were so villainous that Archer felt like he was the hero here.

"If you have the guts, then fight me directly, Dylan! Stop hiding behind others!"

At this moment, Archer stood like a brave warrior, facing off against a final boss villain.

Leah and Lillie exchanged glances, their expressions… complicated.

For some reason, they suddenly felt like they were the bad guys.

"Archer, you're quite the comedian. You're the one in Team Rocket. I'm a Gym Leader who upholds truth, goodness, and beauty."

Dylan shrugged, looking genuinely baffled by Archer's accusations.

"Besides, Champion Lance once told me that when dealing with Team Rocket, any means are justified—because you are Team Rocket, after all."

"That's despicable! Dylan, that's completely immoral!!"

Archer nearly coughed up blood.

Never in his life did he think he'd be the one scolding someone else for using dirty tricks.

"Morality doesn't apply when dealing with Team Rocket. That's also something Lance taught me."

Dylan said this with a straight face.

Lance never actually told him that—but now he had.

And let's be honest, that is how Lance operates. There was no need for morals when dealing with Rocket scum.

"Archer, you're strong. I believe in you. Even if you're facing two fellow executives, you can handle it."

"As long as you defeat them, they'll wake up. Come on, Archer—you can do it."

Dylan genuinely cheered him on, fully invested in this executive civil war.

"???"

Archer's face darkened.

Dylan, thank you so much for your confidence in me. I really appreciate it.

"Proton! Lt. Surge! I swear I'll save you both!!"

Archer clenched his fists and hurled a Poké Ball.

Houndoom and Weezing appeared in front of him.

"Come at me! I will defeat you both!!"

His fighting spirit burned brightly, his eyes filled with unshakable determination.

The battle began!!

But…

"I'm sorry… I was too weak."

Moments later, Archer was on his knees, staring blankly at the still-dancing Proton and Lt. Surge. His voice was hollow.

Fighting both of them at the same time was just too much.

Especially since Lt. Surge was actually stronger than him.

Not only did Archer fail to defeat them, but they weren't even fighting seriously.

"This guy looks like you broke him."

Leah raised an eyebrow, nudging Dylan playfully.

She was so entertained.

This was the first time she had seen a Rocket Executive mentally destroyed like this.

But honestly? Leah loved it.

When dealing with Team Rocket, anything goes!

"He'll be fine. I believe in Archer." Dylan smiled confidently.

"Archer is resilient. He won't break from just this."

Of course not—because if Archer really lost his spirit, who would Dylan visit in prison for fun chats?

PS: Second update today!

---

# 211: Chapter 212: Mew, How Rude!

"By the way, is our hidden guest still not going to show themselves?"

After dealing with Archer and the others, Dylan suddenly raised his head, his lips curling into a smile as he looked toward a seemingly empty spot.

Leah and Lillie blinked in confusion.

"Although your concealment is quite impressive, it's just unfortunate that I have a very keen sense of Pokémon auras."

Dylan waved his hand at Pecharunt.

"Tok-tok-tok~!"

Pecharunt immediately understood, and from its mask, two sinister, poison-coated chains shot out at high speed, striking toward the nearby wall.

Malignant Chain!

However—

That "wall" suddenly moved.

A figure clad in black ninja garb leaped out, skillfully dodging the attack.

"Eh?"

Lillie's eyes widened as she glanced at the now-collapsed gray "wall" on the ground.

So it was just a disguise? She hadn't even noticed!

The masked, middle-aged ninja landed smoothly, his sharp gaze locking onto Dylan and the others.

"You're… Gym Leader Koga?" Leah narrowed her eyes before gasping in shock.

That ninja outfit was way too distinctive—plus, the man's slicked-back hairstyle was a dead giveaway.

Koga, the Gym Leader of Fuchsia City.

One of Kanto's Eight Gym Leaders.

Silence filled the air.

"…I am not Koga."

After a long pause, the man put up a desperate struggle.

Leah gave him a deadpan stare.

Dude, you didn't even bother changing your speech pattern.

"You're still referring to yourself as 'this humble one.' That makes it even more obvious!"

Dylan's expression turned slightly amused.

"Well, I suppose I'll just take off your mask and confirm it myself."

Koga fell into silence again.

"…Fine. As expected of Gym Leader Dylan. I am Koga."

Koga finally exhaled and admitted the truth with a solemn nod.

"I had received intelligence that Team Rocket executives were gathering here, so I came to investigate. I didn't expect to run into you, Gym Leader Dylan."

His voice was deep and composed.

"I see."

Dylan nodded thoughtfully.

"Such dedication… Gym Leader Koga, you came all the way from Fuchsia City to Saffron City, infiltrating Team Rocket's meeting without even informing your own family?"

Koga fell silent once more.

"Although I'd love to take your word for it, I'm afraid your actions are highly suspicious. Would you mind surrendering yourself for questioning?"

"If you're innocent, I'm sure Gym Leader Koga will regain the trust of the League soon enough."

Dylan sighed, his tone patient and understanding.

Koga's brows furrowed. Surrendering was not an option.

He had always heard that Dylan was strong, but Koga was confident in his own abilities.

After all, he was stronger than Lt. Surge.

"But if you're not innocent… I'd advise you not to resist. You have family—unlike Lt. Surge."

Dylan's voice was soft, almost gentle.

Koga's expression flickered slightly before he let out a long sigh.

His daughter, Janine, and his younger sister, Aya, had no idea about his true allegiance.

If he resisted, the League would definitely detain Aya and Janine for questioning.

At least if he surrendered, the investigation on them would be less severe.

Leah's expression darkened.

First Lt. Surge, and now Koga?!

Two official Kanto Gym Leaders were Team Rocket executives?!

It was getting harder not to suspect that there might be more hidden among the remaining Gym Leaders.

Meanwhile, Near the Exit

"Umbreon, Foul Play!"

A sleek, pitch-black Umbreon radiated dark energy as it lunged forward to intercept the charging Slither Wing.

Foul Play vs. Lunge!

BOOM!

Both Pokémon were sent skidding backward, their feet dragging against the ground.

Umbreon lowered its stance, its cold, crimson eyes locked onto Slither Wing.

Slither Wing let out a low growl, flexing its claws aggressively.

"Even without a Trainer, this thing is ridiculously strong."

Karen frowned.

Above her, Flutter Mane hovered ominously, its glowing red eyes watching for an opening—fortunately, her Houndoom was keeping it in check.

But the real problem was—

Behind the two Paradox Pokémon stood a very familiar figure.

"Tok-tok-tok~! Tok-tok~!"

Will was still dancing.

Both Karen and Will's eyes twitched.

"You're still 'Tok-tok-tok'-ing?! Can you STOP?! You look ridiculous!"

Will couldn't take it anymore.

Will's creepy masked dance was a crime against humanity.

"Karen, we can't waste any more time! If Dylan catches up, we're finished."

Will growled.

"I know! Then hurry up and give me some support!"

Karen snapped back, clearly irritated.

She wanted to break through as fast as possible—but Slither Wing and Flutter Mane were too strong.

Short-term, she had no way of knocking them out.

And the longer they stalled—

Dylan would arrive.

"I can't! My Pokémon are already down!"

Will gritted his teeth.

Earlier, he had disguised himself as a low-ranking Rocket grunt and tried to approach Will.

Will didn't hesitate.

Without a single word, he had pulled out Xatu—and all while still dancing—immediately attacking.

Will had barely escaped.

All of his Pokémon had been obliterated in the process.

"We don't even know what happened to Archer's team…"

Will glanced at the three branching paths.

They hadn't seen Archer, Proton, or Lt. Surge in a while…

And then—

"Tok-tok-tok~!"

That same cursed chant echoed behind them.

Karen and Will immediately turned around—

And saw Archer, Proton, and Lt. Surge all swaying to the exact same ridiculous dance.

Their eyes glowed with that same eerie purple light.

"They got controlled too."

Will's expression darkened.

"This bastard… how the hell is he a Gym Leader?! This is pure evil!"

Karen's face contorted.

She wouldn't call herself a good person, but even she wouldn't use such a wicked technique.

Controlling someone's mind?!

This wasn't just immoral—this was borderline villainous.

"Beautiful lady, I can't pretend I didn't hear that."

A crisp, amused voice cut in.

Dylan's clear laughter echoed through the underground base.

Dylan, Leah, and Lillie stepped forward, followed by Koga, who was trailing behind them with a wary expression.

"They ALL got caught…"

Will's stomach sank.

With all the Team Rocket executives gathered here, only Sabrina had managed to escape.

"Gym Leader Dylan, why would such a powerful Trainer concern yourself with us weaklings?"

Karen asked cautiously.

"Miss, you must be joking."

Dylan smiled playfully.

"As a Gym Leader, capturing Team Rocket members is my duty."

"And besides, you're all worth quite a bit in reward money. Why wouldn't I?"

Dylan shrugged.

"Archer and Proton alone earned me some incredible League rewards. Now with even more of you, I'm sure President Goodshow and Champion Lance will be very proud of me."

He smiled brightly.

This time, he had caught so many executives.

Would Giovanni personally come to save them?

Before Karen could retort—

BOOM!

The ceiling shook violently.

A blue energy shield enveloped a figure descending from above.

Dylan's brows lifted slightly in surprise.

As the glowing blue aura dissipated—

A tall, silver-furred, feline-shaped Pokémon landed gracefully before Karen.

Its piercing, psychic-blue eyes locked onto Dylan.

"What kind of Pokémon is that?"

Leah and Lillie both stared in surprise at the sudden intruder.

Its body was sleek and silver-white, with purple eyes, tail, and stomach, and its fingers were composed of three orb-like digits—

Mewtwo.

"Mew?"

As if sensing something, a round, chubby head popped out of Dylan's backpack, tilting slightly as it curiously observed Mewtwo.

Mewtwo's gaze flickered toward the "Clefairy-like" creature—

And its facial muscles visibly twitched.

BEEP—

[Mewtwo]

Type: Psychic | The Genetic Pokémon

Ability: Pressure

Moves: Psystrike, Recover, Psychic, Aura Sphere, Psycho Cut, Safeguard, Earth Power…

Entry: "A Pokémon created through the manipulation of genetic material. While human science was able to create its body, they failed to create a gentle heart."

The Rotom Pokédex displayed the full details of the legendary Pokémon.

"A Pokémon created through genetic manipulation?"

Leah's eyes widened in shock.

"A man-made Pokémon?"

Sure, artificial Pokémon existed—but something genetically engineered like this? That was an entirely different story.

"Leave."

Mewtwo's glowing, psychic-blue eyes briefly glanced at the Rotom Dex before lifting its arm.

Instantly, a blue aura enveloped Slither Wing and Flutter Mane, suspending them in midair.

Under the immense power of Mewtwo's psychic energy, the two Paradox Pokémon couldn't even struggle.

Its deep, echoing voice rang through Karen and Will's minds.

The two Rocket Executives froze.

Then—realizing that Mewtwo was here to help them escape—they wasted no time dashing toward the exit.

Dylan, however, simply raised an eyebrow.

"You barge in here and try to take my prey? Don't you think you're being a bit arrogant, my friend?"

Despite his words, he wasn't actually angry.

If anything, he was far more intrigued by Mewtwo itself than by Karen and Will.

"I don't need them. I'm not your opponent."

Mewtwo's eyes locked onto Dylan.

It could feel it—

Dylan had many auras that made it feel uneasy—some even downright terrifying.

It had come here to assist Giovanni in retrieving his subordinates, but…

Mewtwo had no intention of throwing itself into unnecessary danger.

It wasn't willing to risk its own safety just for Team Rocket's sake.

Instead, at this moment, it had a far greater concern.

"Stay away from me!!"

Mewtwo's eyes flared with blue energy, its telepathic voice filled with unmistakable rage.

Because at some point—

That round, chubby, clearly soft figure had floated directly in front of it.

And its beer belly was right in its face.

Mewtwo immediately felt uneasy, its thoughts clouded with an inexplicable frustration.

"Do NOT come near me!!"

"Mew~! You're so aggressive! But this is weird, why do you smell like me?"

Mew blinked, looking genuinely puzzled, though it was also a little hurt by Mewtwo's hostility.

Mewtwo's face twitched even harder.

"Because Mewtwo was created using your genetic material as a base," Dylan chuckled.

"That's why it's somewhat similar to you. Your DNA exists within its genes."

Mewtwo fell silent.

"Huh?"

Mew's eyes widened, its expression suddenly lighting up with realization.

"So that means you were born from my DNA?!"

"That makes you my child, Mew~!"

Mew practically glowed with excitement, eyes sparkling as it eagerly gazed at Mewtwo.

It didn't care that humans had used its genes to create a new Pokémon.

To Mew, the birth of a new life was never an accident—it was fate.

Rather than dwell on how it came to be, Mew was far more interested in who Mewtwo was as an individual.

If Mewtwo was born from its genetic material, then wouldn't that make it Mew's child?

With pure joy in its voice, Mew opened its arms—

"Come, call me Momma, Mew~!"

"STAY AWAY FROM ME!!"

Mewtwo visibly trembled, its entire body shaking with pure frustration.

A massive psychic shockwave erupted from its body, sending Mew flying backward.

However, Mew effortlessly stabilized itself in midair.

"Fine, fine, no need to be so grumpy, Mew~! But seriously, you're way too extreme!"

Mew pouted, clearly offended by its "child's" rejection.

"Why are you so against me?! I'm adorable!"

Mewtwo's psychic glow dimmed as it quickly regained its composure.

Its cold eyes fixated on the fat, floating cat before it.

"I have no mother. I have no father."

"And I reject the notion that I was created from your genetic material."

Mewtwo's voice was calm, but forceful—

Every word carried undeniable weight.

"Even if I do possess some of your DNA… I refuse to acknowledge it."

Its glare intensified.

"Because there is NO WAY my mother is a fat, neckless pig!!"

Mewtwo practically spat the words out, as if they physically pained it to say.

Silence.

Absolute silence.

Leah: "Pfft—!!"

Lillie: (—)

Dylan: "…"

Even Koga, who had remained serious and alert the whole time, now had a very, very strange expression on his face as he eyed the floating Mew.

It took a few moments—

But finally—

"YOU—YOU—YOU—!!"

Mew stuttered in fury, too enraged to even form words.

"HOW DARE YOU CALL ME A PIG?! YOU'RE SO RUDE! SO INCREDIBLY RUDE, MEW~!!"

"AND WHO SAID I DON'T HAVE A NECK?! MY NECK IS RIGHT HERE!!"

Mew turned in the air, dramatically puffing out its body to emphasize what was, admittedly, a barely noticeable neck.

Mew was livid.

So angry, in fact, that it completely dropped its usual cutesy speech patterns.

It was 1000% done.

---

# 212: Chapter 213: UwU

Mew was furious.

If Dylan made fun of its weight, that was fine—it could admit that it had gotten a little… rounder… over the years.

Just a little.

But getting mocked by a Pokémon that was born from its own genetic material?!

This was the ultimate betrayal!

Wasn't this the equivalent of a child cursing their own parent?!

And worst of all—

It said Mew had no neck!

That wasn't just an insult—that was a personal attack!

"Look closely! I DO have a neck, Mew~!"

Mew was absolutely fuming.

A bright white glow enveloped its body, and in the next moment—

It transformed into its original form.

A small, pink feline—a long, whip-like tail twice the length of its body—

Yes, that was what it was supposed to look like.

But right now?

Dylan opened his mouth, but no words came out.

Because what stood before him was NOT the Mew he knew.

Instead of being small and delicate, Mew was… massive.

Thick.

Its once petite frame was now round—

No—barrel-shaped.

From its chest down to its rear, it was an almost perfect cylinder—

Just… thicker at the bottom.

Dylan was stunned.

If he had to describe it, Mew looked exactly like one of those cats raised by grandmothers, the kind you see online—

Overfed, spoiled, and so fat that its body had become a perfect tube.

Mewtwo stared blankly at the pink barrel before it, its glowing blue eyes frozen in disbelief.

"See?! I HAVE a neck, Mew~!"

Mew proudly pointed to its barely visible neck with its tiny paw.

Its telepathic voice was filled with satisfaction.

Silence.

Mewtwo was… reconsidering everything.

Leah, meanwhile, stared at Mew in shock.

"Wait—THAT'S Mew?!"

Compared to its previous Clefairy-like form, its real body looked even more like a pig!

"Strictly speaking, yes." Dylan nodded.

Lillie: (—)

BEEP—

[Mew]

Type: Psychic | The New Species Pokémon

Ability: Synchronize

Moves: Psychic, Aura Sphere, Transform, Imprison, Metronome, Nasty Plot, Frenzy Plant, Blast Burn, Hydro Cannon…

Entry: "Many believe it is the ancestor of all Pokémon. It possesses immense intelligence and can use any move."

The Rotom Dex dutifully provided Mew's information.

Lillie blinked.

"That's… a lot of moves…"

She was stunned by the sheer number of abilities Mew had.

"Mew is very special," Dylan explained.

"While it may not actually be the ancestor of all Pokémon, it can indeed learn almost every move."

In the games, Mew wasn't truly able to use all moves—

There were still exclusive moves and pseudo-signature moves it couldn't learn.

But according to its Pokédex entry, Mew was meant to be capable of learning every move—

And indeed, in the games, it could learn every single TM and TR move.

That alone was proof that its ability to use all moves wasn't just a myth.

But right now, Mew wasn't interested in any of that.

It was still fuming at Mewtwo.

"Speechless, aren't you, Mew~? Hmph~!"

Mew flicked its tubby tail smugly.

Mewtwo took a deep breath, trying to suppress the mental chaos inside its mind.

And then—

"Let it be known," Mewtwo's voice echoed in the air—

"I, Mewtwo, have absolutely NOTHING to do with this… 'pig'!"

"Not today! Not EVER!"

Mewtwo declared this with absolute conviction—as if trying to cut ties before the universe itself could bind them together.

It was born into this world alone, without bonds, without attachments—

And that was how it intended to stay.

Before, it had struggled with its own existence, questioning if it was just a genetic clone with no true identity.

But now—

It had finally understood.

It was NOT Mew's offspring.

It was NOT a clone.

It was simply Mewtwo.

Mewtwo had finally found its purpose—and it was to have nothing to do with this ridiculous creature.

Dylan watched in amusement, holding back laughter.

Mew blinked, processing what Mewtwo had just said.

"Huh?! That's so RUDE! And how can you say we're not related?! Your genes came from ME, Mew~!"

Mew pouted, crossing its tiny stubby arms.

"No. We are not related." Mewtwo's voice was cold.

"Yes, we are! You even admitted it earlier, Mew~!"

"No."

"Yes!"

"No."

Mewtwo's eyes flickered, glancing at Mew's round belly.

"Our body types are NOTHING alike."

Mewtwo's voice was laced with contempt.

Mew's ears twitched in fury.

"THAT'S BODY-SHAMING, MEW~!"

Mew was so angry that it nearly burst into tears.

"That's IT! I'm gonna FIGHT you, Mew~!"

Mewtwo narrowed its eyes.

"Fine!"

At this moment, Giovanni's orders were completely forgotten.

Mewtwo's eyes glowed a deep blue, its body radiating an intense psychic aura.

It had long wanted to test itself against Mew.

"When a child misbehaves, it's a parent's duty to discipline them, Mew~!"

Mew's voice was firm, its motherly instincts fully activated.

Mewtwo… laughed coldly.

It wasn't Mew's child—

But it would gladly beat the sense out of this ridiculous pink ball!

Before they could clash, Dylan casually interjected.

"Mewtwo, you should show some respect. You've only just awakened, haven't you?"

If Mewtwo's strength was fully unlocked, then sure—

It might have the power to stand against Mew.

But right now?

Mew had been around for centuries, while Mewtwo had barely existed for a few months.

"I may not have full control over my power yet," Mewtwo said calmly.

"But to deal with this… that is more than enough."

Dylan shrugged.

"Good luck, then."

"Just take your fight to the sky. Otherwise, you'll destroy everything here."

Mew and Mewtwo both agreed.

Their psychic energy surged—

Mewtwo's barrier shone deep blue.

Mew's aura radiated a soft pink glow.

Then—

BOOM!

A blue and pink streak shot into the sky, blasting through the underground ceiling and soaring into the open air.

Lillie hesitated.

"Brother, will Mew be okay?"

Unlike the time it fought Zekrom, Mew usually just ate and slept.

Mewtwo, however, looked powerful and determined.

"Don't worry," Dylan smiled.

"At this stage, Mewtwo can't beat Mew."

As for now?

Dylan turned toward Archer, Proton, Will, Lt. Surge and Koga—all restrained and awaiting transport.

"Five Rocket Executives in one night…"

Dylan smirked.

"This was a pretty good haul."

This was a huge win.

Dylan led Archer and the others back to the surface, stepping out into the open air.

Waiting for them was a group of Officer Jennys, and the leading Jenny immediately stepped forward.

"Dylan, excellent work! I can't believe so many Rocket executives were hiding here!"

Her voice was filled with relief and admiration—

Until her gaze landed on Koga.

Her expression froze.

"Koga...?!"

Koga: "..."

Dylan sighed. "Yeah, you're gonna want to interrogate him."

"Understood!"

Jenny quickly signaled her officers, but before they could take all the prisoners into custody—

Her communicator buzzed.

"Ma'am! Two suspects have broken through the north perimeter! We failed to apprehend them!"

Jenny's face darkened.

Dylan wasn't surprised.

Of all the Rocket executives present, Karen and Will were the most dangerous.

Even if the Kanto Elite Four had been here, only Bruno or Agatha would've had a chance at stopping them.

"I apologize, Dylan."

Jenny looked guilty.

"You risked everything infiltrating their base, and we couldn't even secure all of them."

"Don't blame yourself," Dylan said calmly. "If Rocket executives were so easy to catch, they wouldn't have remained a threat for so long."

Sabrina alone was far beyond what most League trainers could handle.

"Still," Dylan added with a smile, "we caught a lot more than we lost. I'd say this is a big win."

Jenny nodded firmly.

"These Rocket executives are extremely dangerous. Leave it to us—we'll personally transport them to Indigo Plateau!"

"I'll leave them in your hands, then."

Dylan patted her shoulder before turning to Leah and Lillie.

"Alright, let's get going—"

But before he could leave, his phone buzzed.

He glanced at the caller ID.

Goodshow.

Wow, that was fast.

Dylan picked up.

The old man didn't even bother with small talk.

"Dylan! Did you just capture another group of Rocket executives?!"

"Yes," Dylan replied casually. "Including Koga."

There was a long pause on the other end.

"...How did this happen?"

In the League headquarters at Indigo Plateau, Goodshow's face was grim.

Koga had been a rising star in the League—

He had even been considered for a Johto Elite Four position once the new League division was finalized.

And now?

He was being arrested as a Team Rocket executive.

Fantastic.

"President," Dylan said, "there may be more to Koga's case. You should speak with him personally. His family likely doesn't know anything either, so I'd suggest handling this carefully."

Goodshow rubbed his temples, feeling a migraine forming.

"The League will conduct a full investigation," he said heavily. "We won't let an innocent man take the fall—but if he's guilty, he'll face justice."

He sighed.

"Still, good work, Dylan. If you ever need anything, let me know."

Goodshow might be exhausted, but he was still deeply relieved.

Dylan was the best thing to happen to Kanto in years.

"I appreciate it, President," Dylan chuckled. "If I need something, I won't hesitate to ask."

"Haha! That's what I like to hear!"

Goodshow laughed before hanging up.

Dylan put his phone away—

And glanced up at the sky.

Above, faint flashes of blue and pink light still flickered—

Indicating that Mew and Mewtwo were still fighting.

"...That's taking a while."

But considering how strong they were, it wasn't surprising.

Then—

DING!

A notification popped up on his trainer chat app.

[Gym Leader Group Chat]

Jenny: @Everyone EMERGENCY ANNOUNCEMENT! Koga, the Fuchsia Gym Leader, has been identified as a Rocket executive! He has been captured and is currently under investigation!

Cerulean Gym - Violet: (shocked Pikachu face.jpg)

Pewter Gym - Flint: ANOTHER one?!

Celadon Gym - Erika: Oh dear… This is truly unexpected.

Jenny: Unbelievable! We let TWO Rocket executives operate as Gym Leaders for YEARS! Thank Arceus Dylan caught Koga today!

Celadon Gym - Erika: Dylan is amazing! He's our best Gym Leader! (fan girl screaming.jpg)

Jenny: @Everyone Alright, listen up! If ANY of you are hiding something, NOW is the time to confess!

Jenny: Seriously, if any of you are still working for Rocket—just turn yourself in now! The organization is crumbling! There's no future in it! (angry face.jpg)

Cerulean Gym - Daisy: We swear we're not in Team Rocket! Right, Lily? Right, Violet?

Cinnabar Gym - Blaine: Haha! Jenny, don't worry. Even if those girls wanted to join Rocket, they wouldn't take them.

Cerulean Gym - Lily: That's so mean, Blaine! (crying emoji)

Viridian Gym - Giovanni: Jenny is absolutely right. I can't believe some would throw away a prestigious Gym Leader position just to become a Rocket grunt. If there are any traitors left, turn yourselves in now. (sighing face.jpg)

Saffron Gym - Sabrina: (thinking.jpg)

Rota Gym - Dylan: UwU.

---

# 213: Chapter 214: Why Do I Always Get Gold?

Outside Saffron City, atop a distant mountain, a man dressed in a black suit and matching fedora silently gazed down at the cityscape.

His expression was grim.

Giovanni.

Behind him, Petrel and Karen stood with uneasy expressions.

Petrel looked like he was about to lose his mind.

"This was supposed to be a simple executive meeting! How the hell did we just lose half our command structure?!"

Out of Team Rocket's four generals, he was the only one left standing.

Everyone else was gone.

"...Boss, what should we do now?"

Karen was also frowning deeply.

She had never expected things to spiral this badly.

This was supposed to be a routine gathering.

Now?

The entire Kanto division was in shambles.

"This is a disaster…"

Unlike the other executives, Karen and Will weren't here for Rocket's sake.

They had infiltrated Rocket on the orders of their master—the Masked Man.

Originally, everything had been going smoothly.

They were talented, intelligent, and ambitious. Giovanni had recognized their potential, allowing them to quickly rise through the ranks.

But now?

Karen glanced at the empty spot beside her.

Will was captured.

Again.

"...How the hell am I supposed to explain this to the boss?"

She rubbed her temples, completely exasperated.

They had barely arrived in Kanto, and within days, they ran into Dylan.

If not for Mewtwo, both she and Will would've been arrested.

And today?

Will didn't get away.

"...That guy has the worst luck," Karen muttered.

Hopefully, prison life would treat him well.

"Karen."

Giovanni's voice snapped her out of her thoughts.

His tone was calm—but carried an undeniable weight.

"...Yes, Boss?"

"Keep an eye on Saffron City. Watch for any police movements. The League will definitely transfer Archer and the others to Indigo Plateau soon."

Karen's eyes sharpened.

"...Understood."

That made sense.

The League wouldn't risk keeping high-ranking Rocket officers in a normal facility.

They'd send them straight to Indigo Plateau.

And no matter how bold Rocket was, raiding the League's headquarters was suicidal.

Even Giovanni wouldn't take that risk.

"Petrel."

"Yes, Boss?"

"You gather information."

Petrel was an expert at disguise—a true master of deception.

If anyone could slip through the cracks, it was him.

"...Understood."

After They Left…

Giovanni remained alone, gazing at the city skyline.

Finally, he sighed.

"...This can't continue."

His time was running out.

He pulled out a small bottle of medicine, swallowed two pills, and turned his back to Saffron City's tallest skyscraper.

"This has to end. One way or another."

His eyes flickered with determination.

He had built Team Rocket from nothing—

And he needed to see how it would all conclude.

Whether it ended in victory or defeat, Giovanni had to try.

BOOM!

A pillar of blue light crashed down beside Giovanni.

A tall, silver-furred humanoid figure stood amidst the fading glow.

Mewtwo.

Giovanni's eyes narrowed.

"...You're injured."

Mewtwo's body was covered in wounds, its breathing was labored, and its aura was unstable.

It had taken serious damage.

Giovanni's voice was calm, but there was an undeniable edge to it.

"...I warned you."

"You're not ready to fight Dylan yet."

But Mewtwo shook its head.

"...It wasn't Dylan."

A pause.

Giovanni studied Mewtwo carefully.

Then, his expression shifted slightly.

"...Then who?"

"...That thing."

A muscle twitched in Mewtwo's jaw.

"...The 'Mew' that follows him."

Giovanni stopped walking.

He turned back to Mewtwo.

"...You lost to Mew?"

Mewtwo's silence was all the answer he needed.

"...I see."

Giovanni's voice remained steady, but deep in his mind, he was already reevaluating everything.

"I told you before, Mewtwo."

He continued walking.

"Mew has existed for countless generations. You have existed for—what? A year?"

"..."

"You're strong—but you haven't even fully awakened your power yet."

"I understand that," Mewtwo said slowly.

"I still don't understand why you never tried to control me."

Giovanni paused.

Then, he smirked faintly.

"Because, Mewtwo… you are my legacy."

"...What?"

"You prove that I was right."

Giovanni's eyes gleamed.

"You are proof that the League's 'morality' is flawed. That true progress requires sacrifice."

"You are undeniable."

Mewtwo stared at him, its eyes flickering with emotion.

It had always struggled with its identity.

But here, in this moment—

It understood.

"...I will fulfill my purpose, Giovanni."

"For now."

Giovanni chuckled.

"You were always free, Mewtwo."

Mewtwo closed its eyes briefly—then turned to leave.

Giovanni watched it go.

Then, he looked up at the sky—

And whispered.

"...Where are you, Silver?"

---

Saffron City – Downtown

Leah furrowed her brows.

"I still can't believe Koga was a Rocket executive."

With two official Gym Leaders exposed as Rocket members, she couldn't help but wonder—

"Are there even more?"

Lillie nodded in agreement.

"It's... unsettling."

She turned toward Dylan.

"Dylan, what do you think?"

Dylan simply shrugged.

"It's not really surprising. The Kanto League is massive. Of course, it's been infiltrated."

He glanced at Leah and Lillie before continuing.

"Look, no matter how righteous an organization claims to be, the bigger it gets, the more corruption it breeds."

The larger the system, the more cracks there were for people like Rocket to slip through.

Even the League—one of the most powerful organizations in the world—had its flaws.

"Think about it," Dylan continued, "even Chairman Goodshow—as kind as he is—has had to deal with his own League's politics."

"And even he's had biases—especially when it comes to his family."

Despite that, Goodshow was still one of the greatest men in League history.

Leah sighed.

"I just want Rocket gone."

Dylan smiled faintly.

"They will be."

Rocket's days were numbered.

Giovanni was reaching his limit.

And when he fell—

Rocket would fall with him.

A Familiar Voice

"Hey! Dylan!"

Dylan paused.

That voice...

He turned his head—

And there he was.

A young boy in a red cap, a bright grin, and a yellow Pokémon perched on his shoulder.

Ash Ketchum.

Reunion

Ash's smile widened.

"It is you! Man, it's been a while!"

"Pika~chuu!"

Pikachu happily waved its little arms.

Dylan blinked—then chuckled.

"Yeah. It has been a while."

He patted Pikachu's head, enjoying the familiar static that tingled against his palm.

"...Still as adorable as ever."

Pikachu purred happily at the attention.

Ash beamed.

"You're so famous now, Dylan! Everyone's talking about the Rota Gym!"

Misty nodded with a grin.

"Seriously, it's like every other League trainer is talking about challenging you."

Dylan laughed.

"You guys are exaggerating."

Brock chuckled.

"They're really not."

Dylan shook his head before gesturing to the two girls beside him.

"Anyway, let me introduce you. You remember Leah, right?"

Leah grinned.

"Long time no see, Ash."

Ash grinned back.

"And this," Dylan gestured to the platinum-haired girl, "is Lillie. My sister."

Lillie gave a polite smile.

"Hello, it's nice to meet you."

Ash blinked—then blurted out:

"Wow. You're really pretty."

Leah immediately elbowed him.

"Idiot."

Ash rubbed his ribs sheepishly.

"Heh... I meant it as a compliment!"

Lillie giggled lightly.

"Thank you."

A Challenge

Dylan crossed his arms.

"So, what are you three doing in Saffron?"

Ash puffed up his chest.

"We're heading to Cinnabar Island to challenge Blaine!"

He grinned proudly.

"I already have six badges!"

Dylan nodded approvingly.

"Not bad."

Ash's eyes suddenly gleamed.

"Hey, Dylan! Can I challenge you?"

Leah and Brock froze.

Even Lillie blinked in surprise.

"...Ash," Misty groaned, pinching her nose.

"You do realize who you're talking to, right?"

Dylan raised an eyebrow.

"You sure about that?"

Ash grinned confidently.

"Yeah! I really wanna battle you!"

Dylan chuckled.

"...Alright."

Ash fist-pumped.

"Yes!!"

"But," Dylan smirked, "we're doing this Rota Gym style."

He gestured to Lillie, who stepped forward, holding a small spinning wheel.

"This has seven different teams. Spin the wheel and see which one you get."

Ash's eyes sparkled.

"Whoa! That's so cool!"

He pressed the button eagerly.

The wheel spun rapidly.

Misty and Brock leaned in.

"Man, I hope he gets a favorable type," Misty murmured.

"...Preferably something easy," Brock agreed.

The wheel slowed down—

And stopped.

Ash blinked.

"...Dylan, what's this gold one mean?"

Misty and Brock's expressions turned stiff.

Lillie and Leah both froze.

Dylan's eye twitched.

"...That's the Legendary Team."

Ash's eyes lit up.

"Whoa! I got a Legendary team?! That's so lucky!"

Dylan sighed.

"...Ash. Spin again."

Ash pouted.

"Aw, why?"

"Trust me."

"...Fine."

Ash pressed the button again.

The wheel spun.

And landed on gold.

Again.

Misty and Brock stiffened further.

Leah tilted her head.

"...Is that thing rigged?"

Dylan rubbed his temples.

"...Ash. One more time."

Ash groaned but obliged.

The wheel spun once more—

And landed on gold again.

"...What."

Dylan and Lillie stared blankly.

Even Leah was speechless.

Lillie slowly turned the wheel around.

She checked the mechanism.

"...It's working fine."

Ash scratched his head.

"Dylan, is your wheel broken? I keep getting gold."

No, Ash.

The wheel is fine.

The problem is clearly you.

"...You and Legendaries have some kind of weird destiny, don't you?"

# 214: Chapter 215: The Third ‘Golden Legend’ Winner

If there was one thing Ash Ketchum had the most fate with, Dylan could confidently say—

It was Legendary Pokémon.

From the main anime to the movies, Ash had encountered Legendaries in every region he traveled.

Some were allies.

Some were foes.

But the fact remained—

Compared to normal people who might never see a Legendary Pokémon in their lifetime—

Ash's luck was downright insane.

But even Dylan hadn't expected this.

A 1-in-7 chance?

And Ash managed to hit it three times in a row?

Gambling Talent?

Leah's eyes shone.

"Ash, have you ever considered playing the lottery?"

She grabbed his shoulders excitedly.

"I'll lend you money!"

"If we win, we split it 70-30!"

Ash blinked.

"Huh? No way! My mom says gambling is bad."

Leah clicked her tongue.

"This isn't gambling…"

"Don't corrupt him."

Dylan sighed and dragged Leah back.

"You really have no shame, huh?"

Leah pouted.

"Ugh, but this is wasted luck!"

A guy who could triple-pull Legendaries shouldn't be wasting it on a gym battle.

He should be rolling for cash!

Ash turned to Dylan.

"So… should I spin again?"

Dylan exhaled.

"...No."

"The rules are the rules. And if you spun again… I have a feeling it'd just be gold again."

Ash grinned.

"Alright! Let's battle!"

Dylan sighed inwardly.

Truthfully, he didn't want to use Legendaries against Ash.

If Ash were at his peak, he wouldn't hesitate—

But right now, Ash was still in his early days.

Unfortunately—

The dice had spoken.

They quickly found a public battle arena.

Dylan and Ash stood at opposite ends.

Leah stepped forward.

"Alright! This is an official challenge for the Rota Gym!"

She raised her hand.

"The battle will be 3v3!"

"Gym Leader Dylan cannot switch Pokémon!"

"If all three of Dylan's Pokémon faint—"

"Ash wins!"

She smiled.

"Battle—START!"

"Wow!"

Misty's eyes sparkled.

"That was super official."

She turned to Leah.

"You sound like a professional referee."

Leah grinned proudly.

"Heh~! Of course!"

"I'm an official assistant at Rota Gym now! My experience is top-tier!"

Misty nodded.

"Honestly, though…"

She sighed.

"I feel bad for Ash."

Brock rubbed his face.

"Yeah… this is gonna be rough."

Leah shrugged.

"Honestly, no one's ever beaten Dylan yet."

"And at least Ash gets to see a Legendary battle firsthand!"

Lillie smiled.

"Exactly! In a way, he's actually very lucky."

Misty and Brock paused.

"...That actually makes sense."

On the battlefield, Ash clenched his fists.

"Man, I'm so excited, Pikachu! Are you ready?!"

"Pika!!"

Dylan chuckled.

"Since this is your first challenge, I'll give you my official introduction."

He slowly adjusted his collar—

Then slipped on his black gloves.

With a graceful flick of his wrist, he looked up and smiled.

"I am Dylan."

"A multi-type trainer."

"And the Gym Leader of Rota."

Misty and Brock froze.

Leah clapped her hands excitedly.

"Ahhhh! It's here! Dylan's intro speech!"

Even Lillie nodded enthusiastically.

Misty gasped.

"He's so… cool."

Brock nodded firmly.

"The charisma is insane."

Dylan smiled faintly.

"Today, I will be using my Legendary team."

"Ash, do you know about Legendary Pokémon?"

Ash blinked.

"...Um?"

Dylan chuckled.

"As I thought."

"Then allow me to show you—"

Dylan flipped a red Poké Ball into the air.

A burst of white light shot out—

A golden figure emerged.

"Zapdos, battle-ready."

A sharp cry echoed—

A massive, golden thunderbird spread its wings—

Electricity crackled from its body.

Zapdos' intense gaze bore down upon Ash—

Like a judge from the heavens.

"IT'S A LEGENDARY!!"

Misty and Brock jumped in shock.

Misty quickly scanned it with her Pokédex.

[Zapdos – The Electric Pokémon]

"A Legendary bird Pokémon that controls electricity.

It lives in thunderclouds, and lightning increases its power.

It is said to appear during violent thunderstorms."

Brock winced.

"That… sounds terrifying."

Lillie smiled.

"This is actually one of Dylan's weaker Legendaries."

Misty and Brock stiffened.

"…Excuse me?!"

Brock gulped.

"How many Legendaries… does Dylan even have?"

Lillie tilted her head thoughtfully.

"Enough to make two full teams."

Brock and Misty: "TWO TEAMS?!?!"

Ash grinned.

"Whoa! Zapdos looks so strong!"

Instead of fear, his eyes shone with excitement.

"This is awesome!"

Dylan smiled.

"You have a good attitude, Ash."

"Now—show me what you've got."

Ash clenched his fist.

"Alright! Let's go!"

He grabbed a Poké Ball—

And threw it high into the air.

"Bulbasaur! I choose you!"

A small, four-legged Pokémon appeared.

Bulbasaur landed, looking determined—

Until it saw Zapdos.

Bulbasaur's face froze.

Zapdos stared down majestically—

Golden lightning flashing around its body.

Bulbasaur slowly turned to look at Ash.

It was visibly sweating.

(Bulbasaur's Thoughts): …Ash. Are you out of your damn mind?!

Bulbasaur turned back—

Then turned back again.

It blinked twice.

Then slowly shook its head.

It opened its mouth.

"Bulba…?"

(…You serious?)

Ash beamed.

"Alright, Bulbasaur! Let's do this!"

Bulbasaur stared at him.

No. No, I don't think we will.

Pikachu waved an encouraging cheer stick.

"Pika pika~! (Good luck, Bulbasaur!)

Bulbasaur: "..."

Bulbasaur slowly turned toward Pikachu.

It narrowed its eyes.

"WHY DON'T YOU FIGHT IT THEN, YOU ELECTRIC RAT?!"

---

---

# 215: Chapter 216: Smell Like That Big Turkey

Bulbasaur felt a little hurt, but orders were orders. It had to fight, no matter what—it couldn't just back down.

"Great! That's the spirit, Bulbasaur!"

Ash was brimming with fighting spirit, his battle intent burning stronger than ever.

"Bulba…"

Bulbasaur took a deep breath and assumed a battle stance.

"Not bad." Dylan nodded and smiled.

Say what you will, but Ash's fearless spirit in the face of strong opponents was already showing, even at his rookie stage.

Unlike that time with Gary, who needed a whole motivational speech to get fired up, Ash was already burning with determination on his own.

"Bulbasaur, use Razor Leaf!"

Ash took the initiative to attack.

Bulbasaur let out a low cry as two spinning waves of sharp leaves shot toward Zapdos.

A Grass-type attack like Razor Leaf was actually not very effective against the Flying-type Zapdos…

But for Ash, type disadvantages were nothing new. He was used to it—no big deal.

That much was evident from the completely unfazed expressions of Misty and Brock.

Then…

Swish, swish, swish!

The Razor Leaf continuously sliced against Zapdos's body, but Zapdos didn't react in the slightest.

"Huh? Bulbasaur, are you not using enough force?"

Ash scratched his head. It felt like the attack didn't even qualify as a scratch for Zapdos—it wasn't reacting at all.

"Bulba!"

Bulbasaur looked at Ash with a helplessly human-like expression. What do you mean 'not enough force'? I put everything into that attack!

"Alright, let's switch it up! Bulbasaur, use Solar Beam!"

Ash quickly stopped dwelling on it and opted for a stronger move.

Bulbasaur held its breath, its bulb glowing with intense white light as it absorbed energy from the sun. Before long, a powerful Solar Beam shot straight at Zapdos.

A Grass-type Solar Beam.

Boom!

The beam struck the unmoving Zapdos directly, causing a loud explosion.

"Yes! A direct hit!" Ash was thrilled.

But then…

As the dust settled, Zapdos reappeared, looking almost completely unscathed. Its piercing gaze remained sharp, and golden electric arcs continued crackling across its feathers.

"Huh??" Ash was stunned.

"The power gap is too big," Brock said with a sigh.

"They're not even in the same league."

Misty pursed her lips. Sure, the type disadvantage played a part, but the fact that even a direct hit from Solar Beam barely did anything could only mean that the strength difference was just that vast.

Simply put, Bulbasaur and Zapdos weren't even on the same level of existence.

"Ash, that was a great Solar Beam, but unfortunately, it's still lacking in power," Dylan commented with a smile.

"Now then, it's our turn."

"Thunderbolt." Dylan finally gave a command.

"Zapdos!"

A high-pitched cry echoed through the sky as Zapdos crackled with electricity, unleashing a powerful Thunderbolt toward Bulbasaur.

"Bulbasaur, dodge it!" Ash's face changed in alarm.

The little green toad immediately sprang into action—it was quite agile, after all. But Thunderbolt was far faster than it had anticipated.

Boom!

Thunderbolt struck true, electric currents surging wildly as an explosion rang out, thick smoke spreading everywhere.

With a single flap of its wings, Zapdos generated a gust that blew away the smoke, revealing Bulbasaur—now unconscious on the ground.

"So… strong!"

Ash stared blankly at his fallen Bulbasaur, completely stunned.

"Pikachu…" Pikachu was just as dumbfounded.

"Bulbasaur is unable to battle. Zapdos wins," Leah announced after checking on Bulbasaur.

"Ash, show me more of what you've got," Dylan said with a smile, gesturing for him to continue.

"Yes!" Ash's fighting spirit flared up again in an instant.

Bulbasaur's defeat didn't discourage him—instead, it made him even more fired up.

"A legendary Zapdos is just as powerful as expected. In that case, to fight something that flies, I should use something that can fly too!"

Ash glanced at Pikachu, thought for a moment, then decisively threw out his ace Pokémon.

"Charizard! I choose you!"

A Poké Ball opened midair, and an orange Charizard landed heavily in front of Ash.

"Oh? Is this the Charmander from before?"

Leah looked at Charizard with some surprise. In the previous battle, she had already determined that Ash was still far from strong—his talent was clearly not on the same level as Red's.

"But… why is it so small?"

After carefully examining the Charizard, Leah noticed something odd.

Something was off about its size. Normally, Charizards stood at about 1.7 meters tall, and well-trained ones could even grow to two meters.

Take Red's Charizard, for example—it was nearly two meters tall. But this one? Leah estimated that it wasn't even 1.6 meters, making it noticeably underdeveloped.

"Yeah, it doesn't seem like a natural evolution," Lillie also observed, looking puzzled.

"Well… Charizard evolved prematurely," Misty explained awkwardly.

The problem was that this Charizard hadn't evolved through Ash's training. Instead, it had forced itself to evolve in a desperate moment to protect Ash.

"The situation back then was pretty dire," Brock added, briefly explaining what had happened to Leah and Lillie.

"I see… then you'll have to train it well from now on," Leah remarked.

The fact that it had evolved out of sheer willpower to protect its trainer said a lot about its character.

"Still…" Misty's expression turned strange as she looked at Charizard.

"Roar!"

Charizard lazily breathed out a weak flame, letting out a lackluster growl.

"Charizard, focus! Today's opponent is really strong!" Ash quickly called out.

Charizard gave Ash a sidelong glance. You've said that so many times already. Every time it's a 'really strong opponent,' and every time I just wipe them out with a single Fire Blast.

The loneliness of a powerhouse…

But then, Charizard's eyes locked onto its opponent, and its expression suddenly changed.

"ROAR!!"

It let out a fierce bellow at Dylan and Zapdos, its tail flame flaring up violently.

Battle intent: MAX!

Charizard's eyes blazed with intensity as it stared at Dylan.

This human… It's him!

It's him!!

That day, Charizard had stood in the audience and watched with its own eyes as its senior—another Charizard—was brutally defeated by this very human.

It had heard the agonized cries of its predecessor. It had seen its battered, humiliated form.

That moment had left a deep scar on the heart of the young Charmander. It had burned that image into its soul—it would never forget.

It had sworn that one day, it would grow strong enough to defeat this human!

Its predecessor had fallen, but that didn't matter.

The pride of Charizard will be upheld by me!

I am a Charizard now!!

Despite being smaller than most of its kind, Charizard radiated an overwhelming aura at this moment.

"Yes! That's the spirit, Charizard!"

"Pika~ Pikachu!"

Ash had no idea what had just happened, but he could tell that Charizard was fired up, and that was all that mattered.

"Interesting."

Dylan raised an eyebrow, intrigued by Charizard's fiery determination.

"Ash, show me how your Charizard compares to Red's Charizard."

"Watch closely, Dylan!" Ash straightened up confidently.

"Charizard, use Flamethrower!"

"ROAR!!"

Charizard launched a scorching jet of fire at Zapdos.

"Thunderbolt." Dylan smiled faintly.

Zapdos unleashed a surge of electricity—Thunderbolt met Flamethrower head-on.

Then…

"CHARIZARD!!"

Ash's anguished cry echoed through the battlefield as his 'invincible' Charizard collapsed, smoke rising from its body.

At Ash's Feet, Pikachu Was Stunned—That Huge Charizard Just Went Down Like That?

"Charizard is unable to battle. Zapdos wins."

Leah announced the battle result.

"Even Charizard's power wasn't enough?"

Ash recalled Charizard, his face scrunched up in frustration. Charizard was supposed to be his strongest Pokémon!

But no matter—he might have lost Charizard, but he still had his electric buddy!

"Pikachu!"

Ash turned his gaze toward the small yellow mouse at his feet. While Charizard was now his ace, Pikachu was still his most important partner.

Pikachu looked up at the imposing, electrified bird in the sky and gulped nervously.

"Pikachu! I choose you!"

"Pika… Pikachu…"

Pikachu trudged onto the battlefield, glancing back at Ash with an expression of deep grievance, like a wife being abandoned.

"You can do it, Pikachu! You're the best!"

Ash cheered loudly for his electric rodent.

"Pikachu, huh?"

Dylan raised an eyebrow as he observed the small Pokémon.

Ash's Pikachu—now this was interesting. But unfortunately, at this stage, Pikachu was still too weak.

Dylan felt a tinge of regret. If this were the Ash from I Choose You, the movie version with exceptional talent chosen by Ho-Oh, then this fight would be much more exciting.

"Ash, my Zapdos is on a completely different level. Do you really think your Pikachu can match it?"

Ash's confidence in his Pikachu remained unwavering.

"I know my Pikachu is strong! It's just as good as your Zapdos!"

Even though most trainers used Pikachu as a beginner's Pokémon before moving on to stronger ones, Ash had always believed in his first Pokémon.

"Alright, I believe you, Ash." Dylan chuckled and nodded.

"Show me Pikachu's power."

"Alright, Pikachu, let's go!"

Ash's spirit was sky-high.

"Pikachu, use Thunderbolt!"

"Pika… CHUUU!"

Pikachu's cheeks sparked with electricity as it released a powerful Thunderbolt at Zapdos.

"Ash, ordinary Electric-type attacks won't work on my Zapdos."

Dylan shook his head.

The Thunderbolt hit Zapdos directly, but the legendary bird remained unaffected. Instead, the electric energy surged around its body, crackling even more intensely.

"Huh?" Ash was stunned.

"Zapdos can absorb electricity to strengthen itself," Dylan explained casually, then gestured toward his Pokémon.

"Zapdos, use Thunder."

BOOM!

A massive bolt of yellow lightning descended from the sky, striking directly onto Zapdos. The fierce electricity coursed through its body, further stimulating its power.

Then, the Thunder attack surged down toward Pikachu.

"Pikachu, dodge it!" Ash's expression shifted in alarm.

Pikachu nimbly leaped to the side, barely evading the first Thunder attack.

But in the next second—

BOOM!

A second, even larger bolt of lightning crashed down from above.

Pikachu had no chance to escape. The powerful electricity engulfed its small body, sending it flying across the battlefield.

"Pikachu!"

Ash rushed forward, cradling Pikachu in his arms, anxiously checking its condition.

"Pikachu is unable to battle. Zapdos wins."

"Ash's three Pokémon are all unable to continue. Challenge failed."

Leah once again announced the result.

"Completely outmatched, huh?" Misty sighed in resignation.

"It was bound to happen. Dylan is just too strong, even if he was holding back."

Brock glanced at Dylan. Throughout the entire battle, Dylan had barely let Zapdos move, and he had allowed Ash to attack first every time.

"Ash."

"Dylan, I'm sorry—I must've disappointed you."

Ash looked up at the approaching Dylan, feeling a little embarrassed.

"There's no need to apologize. Gym battles are meant to help trainers grow stronger. Losing is just part of the process." Dylan smiled.

"Compared to when I saw you on the S.S. Anne, you've improved a lot."

"Well, a lot of time has passed since then!"

Hearing Dylan's praise, Ash's frustration lifted a little.

"Keep training hard—I believe you won't be any worse than Red in the future."

Dylan looked at the determined young trainer and smiled. A faint green glow appeared in his palm as he placed a hand on Pikachu.

With the healing power of Viridian Forest's blessing, Pikachu quickly regained its energy.

"That's amazing!"

Ash marveled at the sight.

"It's just a special ability," Dylan chuckled.

"You have one too, Ash."

"Huh?" Ash was taken aback. I have a special ability? Since when?

"You'll find out in time. Speaking of which—hmm?"

Dylan suddenly looked up as a pink light rapidly descended from above, landing lightly in his arms.

"Mew… Fighting is so exhausting! I feel like I lost a few pounds…"

A soft, playful voice echoed in Dylan's mind as the glow faded, revealing a relaxed 'Clefairy' lounging comfortably in his arms.

At least, it looked like a Clefairy.

But deep down, Mew was smug—it had just taught a certain 'child' a lesson, and it felt immensely satisfied.

"Whoa! Is that a Clefairy?"

Ash's eyes widened in surprise at the round, pink Pokémon. But after getting a closer look, his expression changed.

"Wait… It's so chubby! Dylan, what are you feeding it?!"

Ash suddenly had an idea—maybe he could feed his Charizard the same thing to help it bulk up! Compared to Red's Charizard, his own seemed a bit… scrawny.

"What do you mean, 'so chubby'?! That's so rude!"

The 'Clefairy' immediately puffed up in anger.

What was with today? First, a disrespectful brat, and now another one calling it fat?!

"I'm just a little round, okay?! What's it to you?!"

"Huh? Sorry!"

Seeing the furious expression of the round-bellied 'Clefairy,' Ash instinctively apologized.

"Hmph! So rude!"

The 'Clefairy' pouted, but suddenly, its tiny nose twitched.

"Wait a second… Why do you smell like that big turkey?"

# 216: Chapter 217: I'll Take You There

The 'Clefairy'—who had just been furious—cooled down just as quickly, now curiously sizing up Ash. And when it mentioned the 'big turkey,' it was, of course, referring to the legendary Ho-Oh.

"Huh?"

Ash looked completely lost.

"Are you sure, Piggy?" Dylan asked, slightly surprised as he turned to 'Clefairy.'

Ash had Ho-Oh's presence on him? That didn't make sense. Sure, Ash had always been extraordinarily lucky—after all, he saw Ho-Oh on his first day as a trainer—but the anime Ash wasn't the Rainbow Hero.

Ho-Oh admired pure-hearted humans, that was true, but in the anime, Ash was never officially chosen as Ho-Oh's champion.

It was only in I Choose You, a parallel universe movie, that an Ash was recognized as the Rainbow Hero. That Ash received the Rainbow Wing, encountered Entei and the other members of Ho-Oh's trio, had Marshadow secretly guiding him, and ultimately climbed Mt. Tensei to summon Ho-Oh.

That was true Rainbow Hero treatment.

On the other hand, anime Ash just had insane legendary encounters—he practically met every legendary Pokémon in every region. So running into Ho-Oh wasn't all that surprising.

"No mistake, Mii~! I know that big turkey well, I'd never mistake its aura."

'Clefairy' blinked its big, innocent eyes.

As a Mythical Pokémon from Kanto, it was deeply familiar with the auras of both Ho-Oh and Lugia.

"Ash, you've seen Ho-Oh before, haven't you?"

Dylan mused for a moment before asking Ash.

Ash was taken aback for a second, then thought back carefully.

"Ho-Oh? You mean that colorful bird Pokémon?"

"That's the one."

"I saw it the day I started my journey."

Ash recalled the radiant, rainbow-colored Pokémon he had glimpsed that day. He even made a promise with Pikachu that they'd go meet that Pokémon again someday.

"Actually, I still have this."

Ash reached into his jacket pocket and pulled out a feather.

In the sunlight, the feather shimmered with iridescent rainbow hues—it was breathtakingly beautiful.

Ho-Oh had dropped it when it flew overhead. Maybe the wind was too strong that day, because it had landed right in front of him.

"The Rainbow Wing!"

Leah's eyes widened in shock—she instantly recognized what it was.

She was all too familiar with that feather.

Back when she escaped the Masked Man's organization, she had stolen both the Rainbow Wing and Silver Wing from him.

That was why the Masked Man had been so desperate to hunt her down.

Truthfully, the Masked Man didn't care much about Leah's betrayal.

But the moment she escaped with those two feathers—he had to take action.

"Huh?"

"That's Ho-Oh's feather, Ash. It seems like you've caught its attention."

Dylan chuckled, eyeing the Rainbow Wing with interest.

The Rainbow Wing, also known as the Rainbow Feather.

In the Pokémon games, it was an item needed to summon Ho-Oh.

Its counterpart was the Silver Wing, which was used to summon Lugia.

In the I Choose You movie, the Rainbow Wing had the same function—but with added lore.

The feather could discern the purity of its holder's heart.

If the person was good and kind, the feather would retain its brilliant rainbow colors.

But if the person's heart was tainted by greed or darkness, the Rainbow Wing would lose its luster and turn black.

And when that happened—Marshadow, the Guide of Shadows, would appear to purge the corruption.

"I don't really get it," Ash admitted, scratching the back of his head.

"Just think of it this way—there's a very powerful Pokémon watching over you, and this feather is proof that you can meet it again," Dylan explained with a smile.

"Ohhh…"

Ash understood now.

"So, Dylan, do you want this?"

Ash held out the Rainbow Wing toward Dylan.

"You can have it if you want."

"You're giving it to me?" Dylan raised an eyebrow.

"Ash, this is Ho-Oh's feather—it's incredibly rare. With it, you can summon Ho-Oh. If you're strong enough, you might even catch it."

"Emmm… But I don't even know how to use it, and it's not like I'm strong enough to catch Ho-Oh, right?"

Ash spoke as if it was the most logical thing in the world.

"If anyone can do it, it'd be you, Dylan. Just let me see it once you've caught it!"

Ash wasn't particularly attached to Ho-Oh.

To him, it was just another Pokémon—one he happened to have seen before, but not one he had any deep connection with.

The world was full of Pokémon, and his journey had just begun. Having one more Ho-Oh or one less Ho-Oh didn't make much of a difference to him.

"…wow!"

That was Ho-Oh! One of the most legendary Pokémon in Kanto!

But Ash was talking about giving it away like it was a Spearow.

Brock and Misty exchanged helpless glances but didn't interfere. It was Ash's decision, after all.

"You never change, Ash."

Dylan's eyes glimmered for a moment, then he shook his head.

"Thanks, but I'll pass on the Rainbow Wing."

He declined Ash's offer.

The Rainbow Wing reacted to its holder's heart.

Dylan didn't consider himself a bad person—but he also didn't think he was pure-hearted enough.

If he took it and the feather dimmed, that'd be awkward.

Besides, only those with a pure heart could actually summon Ho-Oh.

To Dylan, this was just a feather—nothing more.

"This feather belongs to you, Ash. You should try finding Ho-Oh yourself."

"But I don't even know where to look…"

Ash looked a little embarrassed. He didn't even know how to use the feather.

"Just follow your instincts—it'll guide you."

Dylan glanced at the Rainbow Wing in Ash's hand.

Ash nodded thoughtfully.

"Dylan, Dylan! Do you really not want the feather, Mii?"

Suddenly, 'Clefairy's' sweet telepathic voice rang in Dylan's mind—just his mind.

"…What's up?"

"If you do want it, I can go get you one! How many do you need? One feather for a box of canned food sound fair, Mii?"

'Clefairy's' eyes gleamed.

It had just discovered a business opportunity!

If it plucked a whole box of feathers, it wouldn't have to worry about food for a month!

Wait… Were Ho-Oh's feathers this valuable to humans?! This was amazing!

"You… You're not thinking of plucking them straight off Ho-Oh, are you?" Dylan was speechless.

"Of course not! Dylan, how could you accuse me of such a thing?!"

'Clefairy' puffed up, looking scandalized.

"Bird Pokémon naturally shed feathers, okay? That big turkey hasn't molted in ages! I'm just helping it get rid of the old ones so new ones can grow—it should be thanking me, Mii!"

Dylan's eyelid twitched.

Seeing that Dylan wasn't interested in Ho-Oh's feathers, 'Clefairy' immediately switched tactics.

"How about Lugia's feathers instead, Mii~?"

Dylan sighed.

"What would I even do with so many feathers—make a feather duster to smack you with?"

He couldn't help but laugh at the absurdity of the situation.

But then again… maybe it wasn't such a bad idea.

As much as he tried to dismiss it, he had to admit that both Ho-Oh and Lugia's feathers were incredibly rare and valuable.

What if he used them as decorations for the Rota Badges? That could look pretty impressive…

Or maybe… it'd be way too flashy?

Dylan fell into deep thought.

---

With the battle concluded, Dylan decided to treat Ash and his friends to a meal—he was getting hungry himself.

They found a nice restaurant and booked a private room.

At the dinner table, Ash wore a slightly troubled expression.

"Dylan, I really do want to see Ho-Oh again… but it feels like it's really far away."

Following Dylan's advice, he had held onto the Rainbow Wing and let his mind go blank.

Sure enough, he could feel a faint, guiding presence directing him somewhere.

But it felt… so far.

"Oh? You can sense it?"

Dylan's interest was piqued.

"Yeah, but…"

"If you don't mind, I can take you there."

Dylan considered it.

Ho-Oh—the so-called 'big turkey'—was something he was genuinely curious about.

But as for catching it?

He wasn't particularly interested.

Trainers chose Pokémon, sure—but Pokémon also chose their trainers.

And Ho-Oh, especially, was picky.

It only recognized humans with pure and untainted hearts.

Its trio of guardian Pokémon existed solely to judge humanity on its behalf.

As the official lore stated:

"The legend says that when humans once again earn its trust, it will return to the Bell Tower."

Dylan didn't think he had the qualities necessary to earn Ho-Oh's recognition.

And even if he managed to capture it by force, it would likely abandon him sooner or later.

If a Pokémon would just up and leave after being caught—then what was the point of catching it in the first place?

And besides, a Pokémon as legendary as Ho-Oh had intelligence and free will on par with humans.

It wasn't just about raw power—it had its own clear sense of purpose.

"I have a Pokémon that can fly. If it's somewhere in Kanto, we can get there by tomorrow afternoon."

"Really?!"

Ash's face lit up with excitement. This was perfect!

"Of course."

Dylan nodded, though his gaze briefly flickered toward Ash's shadow.

He had already asked Mew, and the little troublemaker had confirmed that something was lurking within it.

So it's true…

Still, Dylan couldn't help but feel a bit regretful.

If Ash was following the storyline of the I Choose You universe, why hadn't he also inherited that Ash's incredible trainer talent?

To be fair, anime Ash wasn't bad at training—he was just a late bloomer.

He needed time, experience, and the wisdom gained from countless adventures.

Every battle, every loss, every victory—they would all eventually shape him into the strongest trainer in the world.

When it came down to it, both Ash and Red were destined to reach the top.

The difference?

One was a prodigy, the other was a slow-burning legend in the making.

"Alright! Thanks, Dylan!"

"No need to thank me—I'm just interested in seeing Ho-Oh for myself."

Dylan chuckled.

After all, it was one of Kanto's most famous legends.

As a Kantonian himself, he wouldn't pass up the chance to meet it in person.

But while Ash was beaming with excitement, Leah's expression turned oddly serious.

She pursed her lips slightly, lost in thought.

Ho-Oh…

(End of Chapter)
