# 222: Chapter 223: Dylan, I’m Challenging You Again!

"A Normal-type team still feels incomplete."

Dylan sat in the backyard of Rota Gym, looking at the three Pokémon before him. In reality, he had several other Normal-type Pokémon as well.

For example, he had Ursaluna, his partner Eevee, and his ace, Terapagos, which also happened to be Normal-type.

Technically, he already had a full team.

But team-building wasn't that simple.

Terapagos was way too overpowered for a Normal-type team meant to face regular challengers. Meanwhile, Smeargle, Porygon, and Happiny were all new and still needed training.

Sure, he could use "growth simulations" to boost their progress, but three Pokémon alone weren't enough to balance the team. And there was no way he'd toss Terapagos into the Normal-type lineup—it just didn't fit.

If it were Silvally, that would be a different story. But Terapagos? Absolutely not.

That Pokémon was meant to stay in his "Golden Legend" team, waiting for a worthy challenger.

Unless, of course, the challenger was some old monster-tier Trainer.

"Hmm… Who else should I add? Maybe I should get a Slaking?"

Dylan pondered the composition of his Normal-type team.

Porygon could pair well with Ursaluna as a Special Attack core, but he still needed a strong Physical Attack core. And Slaking was perfect for that role.

Except for its Truant ability… One of the most frustrating abilities in existence. But honestly, it made sense—Slaking would be way too broken otherwise.

"Slaking with Guts… Would that even be legal?"

He chuckled at the thought.

"If only I had a Ursaring-Hisui form."

Dylan sighed. The original Ursaring-Hisui (Ursaluna Bloodmoon) was an absolute Physical powerhouse—a near-perfect Pokémon, aside from its slow Speed.

Unfortunately, Hisui Ursaring's evolution required a special environment that didn't exist in the modern era. There was no way a normal Ursaring could evolve into Ursaluna now.

"Eh, I'll think about it later."

For now, he was focused on teaching Smeargle more moves with Sketch.

In the games, Smeargle could only learn four moves. But in real life? There were no such limits.

That meant…

"Fake Out—must-have. Parting Shot—must-have. Destiny Bond—must-have. Protect-based moves like Baneful Bunker and Flame Cloak—also must-have."

"Tch… So many essential moves."

Dylan scratched his chin, staring at Smeargle, who was completely confused by its Trainer's deep thinking.

But the real breakthrough came next.

"Dark Void works! HAHA—This is amazing!"

He had Darkrai demonstrate Dark Void, and Smeargle successfully copied it with Sketch.

Nice!

Meanwhile, in Kanto…

With five of Team Rocket's executives captured, the organization suffered a devastating blow.

Officer Jenny, wasting no time, immediately transported the captured executives in separate unmarked vehicles to avoid detection. They were successfully transferred to the Indigo Plateau prison.

Ariana now had five new cellmates.

How wonderful.

Dylan was even considering paying them a visit sometime soon.

After all, Archer and Proton were practically his best friends.

Half a Month Later—Rota Town Entrance.

A boy wearing a cap stood at the town's entrance, staring at the Rota Gym bulletin board.

His crimson eyes gleamed with determination.

"Dylan! I'm challenging you again!"

Rota Gym—Backyard.

"I should prepare a gift for Selene."

Dylan sat on the grass, thinking about a gift for his first apprentice, Selene.

She was about to reach adulthood, and due to her rising reputation at Rota Gym, her mother had apparently abandoned their plans to move to Alola.

In the games, they were supposed to move before Selene set off on her journey.

But now? There were no signs of moving.

Which made sense.

Her daughter was doing so well at Rota Gym—why would she leave?

And as her mentor, Dylan felt obligated to give her a gift to celebrate her official journey as a Trainer.

He already had an idea of what to give.

"Looks like we're not expecting many challengers this afternoon."

Leaf stretched lazily, her curvy figure accentuated by the motion.

Yup. She was back at Rota Gym—once again mooching off free food.

"The Indigo Conference is starting soon. More Trainers have collected eight badges, so they're all in special training mode."

Lillie was replying to messages on her phone. She chuckled at the news.

"Special training?" Leaf yawned.

"Honestly, all those weaklings could train for a month straight and still wouldn't improve as much as if they just challenged Dylan's Gym a few times."

She had completely embraced her identity as part of Rota Gym.

"Leaf, aren't you going to enter the Indigo Conference?"

"Nah."

Leaf shook her head. The tournament would attract too much attention, and she didn't want to risk exposing herself to the Masked Organization.

"Besides, there's no point entering this year."

Leaf's mind drifted to a certain red-eyed boy.

The Indigo Conference champion was already decided.

He wasn't as ridiculous as Dylan, but he was still a monster.

By now, he was definitely at Champion level.

No—he was Champion level.

"After all, I personally picked him as one of my two big powerhouses back then."

"Maybe next year. Selene, you should fight him one day."

Leaf smirked, turning to Selene.

"Monsters should battle monsters, don't you think?"

To her, this was common sense.

"Hehe~" Selene puffed out her chest proudly.

"Yeah, I'm awesome!"

"Eh? Leaf, you know Blue?"

Asha, tilted her head curiously.

She assumed Leaf was talking about Blue Oak.

After all, if Blue participated in the Indigo Conference, the other competitors might as well go home.

"Blue? No, I meant—"

Leaf started, but before she could finish—

CREEAAK—

The doors of Rota Gym swung open.

A cap-wearing boy stepped inside.

"Hello! Are you here to challenge Rota Gym?"

Asha immediately approached, greeting the newcomer with a bright smile.

"Yes."

The boy lowered his cap slightly, his red eyes hidden beneath its shadow.

"Red?"

Leaf blinked.

Red looked up at her in confusion.

"Eh? You two know each other?"

Asha was surprised.

"Hah— I work here now! Red, are you here to challenge Dylan?"

Leaf smiled playfully.

"…"

Red nodded silently.

There were… too many people staring at him.

His social anxiety was kicking in.

"Hehe~ I get to see you battle Dylan again! Nice, nice! Come here, Red."

Leaf enthusiastically led Red toward Rota Gym's famous roulette wheel.

She was super excited to witness Red vs. Dylan—Round 2.

This time, who would win?

A Legendary Team, Huh?

Red lowered his cap slightly.

To be honest… he was a little tempted to try.

And Leaf wasn't wrong—if he wanted the toughest challenge, then the Legendary team was the way to go. A team composed entirely of Legendary Pokémon…

"Go on, Red, let's see what team you land on."

Leaf was already used to Red's silence.

After all, he was naturally the quiet type.

If someone didn't know him, they might even mistake him for a complete shut-in.

Red pressed the button, and the pointer on the roulette wheel spun rapidly before gradually slowing down…

It finally stopped on a red section—with a flame emblem.

"A Fire-type team, huh? Well, that does suit you."

Leaf tapped her chin, feeling a bit disappointed.

Why couldn't it have landed on gold?!

She really wanted to see what kind of expression this nearly emotionless guy would make if he had to face Dylan's Legendary team.

"Fire, huh…"

Red stared at the flame-marked section, lost in thought.

And just then—

"Red."

A surprised yet familiar voice called out from the side.

"Dylan!"

"Red!"

Red immediately turned toward the voice.

A black-clad figure was walking toward him, and the moment Red's scarlet eyes met Dylan's, his expression shifted.

His eyes brightened, and despite himself, the corners of his lips curled into a small, almost imperceptible smile.

"It's been a while."

Dylan stepped forward, smiling at Red.

"It's been about… three months now, hasn't it?"

Dylan recalled their last encounter—it had been back in Celadon City.

Red nodded. He remembered it clearly too.

"I thought you weren't going to challenge me anymore," Dylan teased. "After all, the Indigo Conference is only about a month away."

"Training took a lot of time."

Red actually spoke a full sentence, which was rare for him.

He wanted Dylan to understand—this battle mattered to him.

Dylan was both his rival and friend, someone he deeply respected.

"Oh? Looks like you came prepared this time."

Dylan raised an eyebrow.

Red didn't respond with words. Instead, he simply locked eyes with Dylan—his red pupils burning with intense battle spirit.

He could barely contain himself.

"Looks like whatever we have to say can wait until after the battle," Dylan chuckled.

He was also very curious to see how much stronger Red had become.

And the moment a challenger stepped into the battlefield, three familiar figures entered the spectator stands—right on time.

It wasn't just them—several curious onlookers had also entered the Gym. Rota Gym was becoming more and more popular.

Gym Battle Arena.

Dylan and Red took their places on opposite sides of the battlefield.

Leaf walked into the center, holding up a small flag.

"This will be a Gym Battle for the Rota Badge!"

"The challenger is Red, from Pallet Town."

"The battle format will be a full 6v6 battle! The Gym Leader, Dylan, will send out his Pokémon first and cannot switch Pokémon."

"If all six of Dylan's Pokémon are defeated, the challenger wins!"

"Battle—START!"

Leaf swung the flag down, officially starting the match.

"Whoa? A full 6v6 battle?"

Asha was surprised.

In all the time she had been at Rota Gym, this was the first time she had seen Dylan participate in a full-team battle.

Selene and Lillie were also intrigued.

Everyone knew that Gym Leaders set their own battle rules.

So if Dylan had chosen a full 6v6 battle, then it meant Red was special.

Normally, Rota Gym battles were either 2v2 or 3v3.

Even when Blue challenged Dylan, it had been a 5v5 battle.

This was the first full-team battle ever.

"Well, the opponent is Red, after all."

"Ilene, you're here too?"

Asha and the others turned toward the woman who had just arrived.

"Mhm~ I came to check things out and happened to arrive just in time for Red's challenge."

Ilene smiled gracefully, her elegant demeanor drawing attention as her gaze landed on Red.

She remembered him quite well.

After several months, he had finally come to challenge Rota Gym.

Back in the Arena.

Red turned his gaze toward Leaf.

The battle rules had already been announced—so why was she still standing there?

STARE.

"Red, do your best! Knock Dylan out!"

Leaf winked at Red, giving him a cheerful thumbs-up before finally stepping out of the battlefield.

Scoreboard Lights Up.

On the large electronic scoreboard, Dylan's and Red's faces appeared on opposite sides.

"Red, I've been waiting for this day for a long time."

Dylan gracefully unbuttoned the collar of his black shirt, then slowly slid on his black gloves.

All the while, his gaze never left Red.

"Dylan, this time—I'm going to defeat you!"

Red lowered his cap, his scarlet eyes gleaming fiercely from beneath the shadow.

Defeating Dylan.

Winning the Rota Badge.

These were the thoughts that had occupied Red's mind for months.

And on top of that…

That Damn Blue!

Red's eyelid twitched slightly.

Blue had challenged Dylan first and won the Rota Badge before him.

Ever since then, he wouldn't shut up about it.

Every time Red opened his messages—Blue was flexing.

All. The. Time.

"Hah! Red, guess what? I already beat Dylan! Good luck catching up to me!"

"Yo, Red, how does it feel knowing I got the Rota Badge first? 😉"

"You're not scared, are you? C'mon, step it up, slowpoke!"

"LOL, imagine not having the Rota Badge yet. Couldn't be me. 😆"

Red was so done with this.

Just because Blue was a little faster than him—

He acted like he was the king of the world.

Enough.

Red clenched his fist.

Watch closely, Blue.

I'll beat Dylan and get the Rota Badge too.

If Blue could do it—then so could he.

I will win!

---

---

---

# 223: Chapter 224: Flames Replacing the Sun!

The Bird Keeper in the audience looked at Red in surprise.

There was no doubt—a full-team battle meant Red was strong.

"This guy… Why does he look so familiar?"

The eldest sister in the group crossed her arms, lost in thought.

She was certain she had seen him before.

"Dylan's Fire-type team… We haven't really seen much of it. I wonder how strong it is."

The Psychic trainer was more interested in Dylan's Fire team.

Fire was a popular type, and many Trainers specialized in it.

Would Dylan's Fire-type team be any different?

In the Arena.

"Words like that should only be said after you win."

Dylan spun a Poké Ball between his fingers, smiling at Red.

"This time, I'll be using my Fire team against you."

"Red, experience the true heat of flames."

"My first Pokémon will be—this one."

With a flick of his wrist, Dylan threw out his first Poké Ball.

"Ninetales, battle ready!"

A flash of white light, and a golden-furred, elegant Ninetales landed gracefully in front of Dylan.

A Fire-type Ninetales.

"Ninetales, huh? Blastoise!"

Red's crimson eyes remained unreadable.

Ninetales wasn't exactly rare—it was actually a very popular Fire-type Pokémon.

Both Vulpix and Ninetales were highly sought after due to their beautiful appearances.

However, when it came to battle potential—Arcanine was generally considered superior to Ninetales.

Red tossed his Poké Ball, and with a burst of light, his strong and sturdy Blastoise landed with a heavy thud in front of him.

"Ah, it's that Blastoise again… But this time will be different."

Dylan chuckled softly and snapped his fingers.

"Ninetales."

Ninetales let out a long, melodic cry.

A blazing orb of light rose into the air, and in an instant—

The battlefield was bathed in intense sunlight.

The sky became so bright that looking directly upward became difficult.

Ninetales's ability: Drought!

The weather changed to Harsh Sunlight.

Red remained silent.

He had expected this.

Ninetales with Drought was rare, but not impossible.

And Dylan wouldn't settle for an inferior ability like Flash Fire.

"Well, Red—since you're the challenger, you get the first move."

Dylan gestured elegantly.

"Blastoise! Hydro Pump!"

Red gave no hesitation.

Even under Harsh Sunlight, Water still had a type advantage over Fire.

And at full health, Hydro Pump's power was immense.

Blastoise let out a deep roar—

Its cannons expanded, charging up—

A massive surge of water blasted forth, flooding the battlefield as if a tidal wave had erupted.

The sheer volume of water was overwhelming—practically drowning the entire arena.

"It's gotten much stronger."

Dylan raised an eyebrow.

This Blastoise was far stronger than when he had faced it back in Vermilion City.

But it still wouldn't be enough.

"Baton Pass."

Ninetales's eyes glowed mysteriously.

Its body turned into white light—

Just before the Hydro Pump could hit—

Ninetales disappeared back into its Poké Ball.

The attack completely missed.

Baton Pass.

A Normal-type move that switched Pokémon, transferring any stat boosts along with it.

Though Ninetales hadn't used any buffs, it was still a clean escape.

Red remained silent."Incineroar!"

Dylan threw out another Poké Ball.

With a burst of light, a muscular, tiger-like Pokémon landed in front of him.

Incineroar bared its fangs, spreading its arms wide.

Its eyes locked onto Blastoise, a wicked grin spreading across its face.

Intimidate!

A white aura flickered around Blastoise, lowering its Attack stat.

"Incineroar, huh…"

Up in the stands, several spectators frowned.

Incineroar had gained a reputation for being an absolute menace in battle.

Recently, more and more Trainers were traveling to Alola just to get one for themselves.

It had become infamous.

"Hydro Pump again!"

Red wasted no time.

"Fake Out."

Dylan smirked.

Incineroar blurred forward—

PAH!

It slapped Blastoise across the face.

Blastoise flinched—its attack canceled.

"Parting Shot."

Before Blastoise could react—

Incineroar pointed at it, muttering some very colorful insults.

Blastoise's expression darkened.

It looked incredibly pissed off.

Its Attack and Special Attack dropped.

Then—

Incineroar vanished back into its Poké Ball.

"…"

Red was completely silent.

Since when did Dylan fight like this?

"Volcarona, battle ready!"

Dylan's third Pokémon took the field.

A large, moth-like Pokémon hovered in the air.

Its blue eyes gleamed coldly as it stared down Blastoise.

A heavy, oppressive heat filled the battlefield.

Volcarona.

Red's expression grew serious.

This Volcarona was strong.

"Beep—Volcarona, Bug/Fire-type, the Sun Pokémon."

Ability: SwarmMoves: Fiery Dance, Fire Blast, Hurricane, Bug Buzz, Flamethrower, Heat Wave, Quiver Dance, Morning Sun, U-turn…

Pokédex Entry: When the land is shrouded in volcanic ash, Volcarona's flames serve as a substitute for the sun.

"This thing looks insanely strong!"

The eldest sister in the audience was shocked after reading its Pokédex entry.

"'Volcarona's flames serve as a substitute for the sun'?!

That was way too overpowered.

And this was Dylan's Volcarona.

"Volcarona is Champion Alder's ace Pokémon…"

The Psychic trainer muttered.

"How does Dylan's compare to Alder's?"

"With Harsh Sunlight, Fire moves will be even stronger."

"And good thing the challenger is using Water-types."

"But Volcarona has a 4x weakness to Rock."

The Bird Keeper nodded.

Fire/Bug had a massive weakness to Rock moves.

And Blastoise knew Rock Slide.

"But Blastoise's Attack was lowered twice already."

"If Red wants to take down Volcarona, he might need to switch."

In the Arena.

"Blastoise, Hydro Pump!"

Red didn't switch.

He wanted to test Volcarona's strength first.

Even with its stats lowered, Blastoise wasn't afraid of a Fire-type.

It was also a good way to gather information.

Blastoise's cannons glowed blue—

"Fire Blast."

Dylan smirked.

Volcarona remained still.

A massive flaming kanji "大" (Big) formed before it—

Blastoise's Hydro Pump and Volcarona's Fire Blast collided in mid-air.

Classic Water vs. Fire.

Blue and Red clashed violently.

BOOM!

Blazing Flames vs. Raging Waters!

Scorching flames and icy water clashed violently, red and blue light intertwining in a spectacular battle.

Yet, to everyone's shock—

Hydro Pump failed to overpower Fire Blast.

In fact, Volcarona's Fire Blast was slowly pushing Hydro Pump back.

"It's useless, Red."

Dylan's lips curled into a smug grin.

"Even Hydro Pump can't stop my Volcarona!"

Even against Red's Blastoise, it didn't matter.

With Harsh Sunlight active, Fire-type moves gained extra power, while Water-type moves were weakened.

Plus, Blastoise's Special Attack had already been lowered by Incineroar's Parting Shot.

What could it possibly do against Volcarona?

If not for Hydro Pump's naturally high power, Fire Blast would have already scorched Blastoise's shell.

"Blastoise!"

Red's crimson eyes sharpened.

He calmly issued a command.

Blastoise let out a deep roar.

Its Hydro Pump surged, intensifying its force against Fire Blast.

BOOM!

The two attacks finally lost balance and exploded.

Fiery embers and steam erupted, sending powerful shockwaves through the battlefield.

"Volcarona."

Dylan's voice was steady.

Volcarona flapped its six blazing wings, effortlessly floating above the aftermath.

Tiny embers danced around its body like fire spirits.

Moving with an almost hypnotic grace—

Volcarona glided through the fiery wind, arriving right above Blastoise.

"Blastoise! Rapid Spin!"

Red's expression darkened.

Something wasn't right.

Blastoise tucked its limbs and head into its shell, spinning at high speed.

"Fiery Dance!"

Volcarona's wings flared.

The floating embers ignited instantly, transforming into a rain of flames.

Fiery Dance!

Once, Fiery Dance had been Volcarona's signature move.

Now, Iron Moth could also learn it—so technically, it wasn't exclusive anymore.

Still—

On this battlefield, under this sun…

Volcarona was the only one dancing in the fire.

A sea of crimson flames cascaded downward.

Volcarona twirled midair, weaving gracefully through the embers, as if performing a ritualistic dance.

The sight was—

Mesmerizing.

"Blastoise!"

Red's gaze sharpened.

Blastoise's sturdy shell could withstand physical blows.

But against pure elemental power?

Not as well.

Blastoise spun faster, enduring the searing flames while trying to escape the attack.

The scorching heat licked at its shell, but Blastoise pushed through, fighting to break free.

"Blastoise! Water Gun!"

Red issued a sharp command.

Still spinning, Blastoise's cannons glowed blue—

Then—

A high-pressure water jet blasted outward, clearing a temporary path through the flames.

Blastoise burst through the inferno, emerging from the attack's range.

But Volcarona was already on the move.

"Fiery Dance—again."

Dylan's voice was calm but firm.

Volcarona soared higher—

Then—

Another wave of fiery embers rained down.

More Fiery Dance!

BOOM!

Flaming projectiles collided against Blastoise's shell.

The explosion sent Blastoise flying, knocking it out of its spin.

Blastoise crashed onto the ground, its limbs splayed out, panting heavily.

Red frowned.

Meanwhile—

Volcarona's body began to glow faintly.

A soft aura of flames flickered around it.

Its Special Attack had increased!

Fiery Dance's secondary effect had activated!

Now—

Floating high above the battlefield, backlit by the blazing sun, Volcarona looked almost like a miniature sun itself.

From above, it gazed down at Blastoise, its pure blue eyes cold and indifferent.

Even as smoke curled from Blastoise's scorched shell, Volcarona remained still.

But its presence alone was suffocating.

Even as a spectator, you could feel its overwhelming power.

"That… That was insane…!"

The eldest sister in the audience finally snapped out of her daze.

Her voice trembled slightly.

"What kind of monster is that?!"

From start to finish, Volcarona had only used Fire-type attacks.

Yet—

It had completely dominated Blastoise.

Even though Bug-type moves weren't resisted, Dylan had chosen to rely only on Fire.

This was a statement.

A declaration—

"I will defeat you with Fire alone."

"No…"

The Psychic trainer shook his head.

"Volcarona is incredibly strong, but its dominance here is also due to the weather advantage—and Blastoise's lowered stats."

During the first Fire Blast vs. Hydro Pump clash, it was already clear—

Volcarona wasn't significantly stronger than Blastoise.

Their levels were close.

"The sunlight won't last much longer."

The Psychic trainer glanced at the sky.

The artificial sun created by Ninetales was beginning to fade.

But until it did—

Blastoise had no chance to recover.

---

---

---

# 224: Chapter 225: Insane

"Red, how's my Volcarona?"

Dylan spoke slowly, smiling as he looked at Red.

Since he was battling Red, he had to get serious. Dylan enjoyed facing strong challengers—he loved battling, especially against opponents who could ignite his fighting spirit.

"Very strong."

Red, in battle mode, was much more talkative than usual, and the burning intensity in his eyes showed his emotions at this moment.

"Come back, Blastoise."

Red recalled his Blastoise.

Although it could still fight, it had been weakened significantly by Incineroar. Continuing the battle would be meaningless.

"Lapras!"

Red threw out his second Pokémon.

With a low cry, a Pokémon resembling a plesiosaur landed before him. Its body was primarily blue, with a gray shell on its back covered in uneven protrusions.

It was Lapras, a Water/Ice-type Pokémon.

Beep—Lapras, Water/Ice-type, the Transport Pokémon.

Ability: Shell Armor

Moves: Sheer Cold, Perish Song, Hydro Pump, Rain Dance, Life Dew, Surf...

Description: With a kind heart that understands human speech, it carries people across the sea on its back.

"This Lapras is really strong too."

The younger girl clicked her tongue in amazement. At this point, it was clear—Red's strength was definitely on par with Blue's!

"Yeah, seriously impressive. Another monster."

Lillie was equally astonished. Trainers from Pallet Town all seemed ridiculously powerful.

Red had just started his journey this year, right? And he was already at the Champion level? And then there was Blue—another monster.

Ash might not have the same raw talent as these two, but could someone who had caught the attention of Ho-Oh really end up being weak? Lillie thought that was impossible. Her brother also treated Ash differently, which was another sign.

And then there was Leaf. She was originally from Pallet Town, too.

Trainers from Pallet Town were just absurdly strong!!

"Mm-hmm~ No worries, Selene. You're not any worse than them, and Yellow will catch up to them too." Leaf smiled playfully.

"Of course!"

"Huh? I'll do my best..."

This clearly showed the difference in personality between Selene and Yellow.

On the Battlefield

"Red, Water/Ice-types don't resist Fire, you know."

Dylan studied Lapras. This one was exceptionally well-developed. Normally, a Lapras around 2.5 meters was considered standard.

But Red's Lapras was at least 2.8 meters tall—its training had been meticulous.

"..."

Red remained silent.

Water resisted Fire, but Ice was weak to Fire, so Lapras didn't actually resist Fire-type moves. However, its Water-type attacks were still super effective against Volcarona.

Besides...

"Lapras, Surf!"

Red gave the order without hesitation.

A deep blue glow shimmered around Lapras as its clear cry rang out.

Whoosh—whoosh—whoosh!

The sound of waves echoed as a towering wave surged up from the ground, lifting Lapras and crashing toward Volcarona.

A Water-type Surf!

"Fiery Dance."

Dylan smiled calmly.

Volcarona slid backward gracefully, its wings igniting with flickering flames. As it flapped powerfully, fiery waves shot forward, colliding with the Surf attack.

Fire-type Fiery Dance!

Water and fire clashed violently, sending steam billowing throughout the arena, making the temperature rise rapidly.

The referee, seeing this, immediately opened the gym's ceiling panels to release the excess heat—otherwise, the temperature inside would become unbearable.

"Body Slam!"

Red's red eyes gleamed sharply.

From atop the surging wave, Lapras leaped high into the air, diving straight for Volcarona.

A Normal-type Body Slam!

"Retreat, Solar Beam."

Dylan glanced at the sky. The Sunny Day effect was about to fade—not good news for him, since it greatly benefited Fire-types.

Volcarona quickly flapped its wings and retreated while its glowing wings absorbed sunlight.

A Grass-type Solar Beam!

Boom!

A blinding white beam of energy shot toward Lapras.

"Ancient Power!"

Red reacted instantly.

Lapras let out a powerful cry as multiple glowing stones materialized and shot toward Volcarona.

A Rock-type Ancient Power!

Dylan raised an eyebrow. So this was why Red had brought Lapras so close to Volcarona.

The Solar Beam struck Lapras directly, causing an explosive shockwave. Lapras cried out in pain as it was blasted backward.

Being a Water/Ice-type, Lapras was weak to Grass—this hit dealt serious damage.

But at the same time, the Ancient Power boulders slammed into Volcarona, making it screech in pain as it was sent crashing to the ground.

Volcarona was quadruple weak to Rock-type moves! The damage from Ancient Power was immense.

"That was amazing! That Ancient Power hit hard!"

The birdkeeper's eyes lit up. As a challenger, he was, of course, rooting for Red.

"Truly impressive. He must have planned that in advance. That Ancient Power was timed so it wouldn't collide with Solar Beam. Otherwise, Solar Beam would've completely obliterated it."

The older girl sighed in admiration. That was the brilliance of the move.

If Lapras had fired Ancient Power head-on against Solar Beam, it would have been overpowered instantly.

Now, though, even though both Pokémon were heavily injured, Ancient Power was quadruple effective against Volcarona. This was an acceptable trade for Lapras.

After all, Volcarona was too overwhelming—knocking it out, even at the cost of Lapras, was absolutely worth it.

"As expected of you, Red."

Dylan wasn't too surprised as he glanced at both downed Pokémon.

While Ancient Power was quadruple effective, Volcarona had been in peak condition and had excellent Special Defense.

"Unfortunately, it just wasn't strong enough."

Despite the heavy damage, Volcarona slowly lifted itself off the ground, wings trembling.

Its once-calm blue eyes were now cold and furious as it glared down at the struggling Lapras.

Lapras had dealt a major blow with Ancient Power, but it hadn't been enough to take Volcarona down completely.

"Your Volcarona is the same."

Red looked at Lapras. It had suffered severe injuries—the super-effective Solar Beam had hit hard, and Volcarona's Special Attack had already been boosted once.

But thankfully, Lapras had been trained exceptionally well—it could still hold on.

"That's why I said—I'm really looking forward to your challenge, Red. You're exactly the kind of opponent I love!"

Dylan was thrilled by the unpredictability of such a powerful opponent.

A yellow-green energy began to shimmer around Volcarona.

This was Bug-type energy—the activation of its ability, Swarm!

Swarm: When a Pokémon's HP is low, its Bug-type moves become more powerful.

Essentially, it was the Bug-type equivalent of Blaze.

"Volcarona, Bug Buzz!"

A brilliant energy radiated from Volcarona's body as a piercing, discordant buzzing sound filled the air. Sonic waves pulsed outward in tangible waves.

A Bug-type Bug Buzz!

Sunny Day had faded, but now Swarm was in effect.

"Lapras, Protect!"

Red's voice was firm.

Despite the deafening noise, Lapras endured it, summoning a shimmering green barrier.

The sonic waves crashed against the Protect barrier but were completely blocked.

"You can't Protect forever, though, can you?"

Dylan chuckled knowingly.

Volcarona let out another Bug Buzz, its sonic attack rippling through the air.

With its aerial advantage and Lapras' relatively slow speed—especially on a standard battlefield where mobility was further restricted—Lapras had little room to maneuver.

As for trading blows head-on? There was no way Lapras could win a direct clash against a Swarm-boosted Volcarona.

"Surf!"

Red quickly made his decision, giving Lapras the command.

Once again, a massive wave surged from the ground, carrying Lapras as it rushed toward Volcarona.

However, the Bug Buzz sound waves struck the water, vibrating through the wave and shattering it from the middle, causing the Surf to collapse.

The power of Bug Buzz was overwhelming!

But before the wave could fully dissipate, Lapras had already leaped high into the air—its target, Volcarona.

"You think the same move will work twice, Red? That's underestimating me a little, don't you think?" Dylan raised an eyebrow.

"Volcarona, Hurricane!"

Volcarona let out a deep cry. As it flapped its wings forcefully, a violent storm surged forward to engulf the descending Lapras.

A Flying-type Hurricane!

Dylan wasn't about to let Lapras trade hits this time. Hurricane's wide coverage attack was completely different from the previously used Solar Beam.

"Protect!"

Red reacted instantly.

As Lapras plummeted toward Volcarona, a green Protect barrier shimmered around its body, blocking the raging winds of Hurricane.

The Protect barrier began to crack.

"Bug Buzz!"

"Brine!"

At the moment Protect shattered, both Pokémon attacked simultaneously—Volcarona unleashed another Bug Buzz, while Lapras fired a jet of water.

A Water-type Brine! When the opponent's health was low, Brine's power doubled!

The sonic waves of Bug Buzz pulsed outward, covering the entire space in front of Volcarona.

"..."

Red had intended to make Brine slip past Volcarona's attack like before, but Dylan had anticipated this.

With Bug Buzz covering such a large area, Brine couldn't sneak through—it had to clash head-on with the attack.

However, with Volcarona's Special Attack boosted and Swarm activated, its Bug Buzz was absurdly powerful.

Even at double power, Brine couldn't break through—it was Bug Buzz that struck Lapras instead.

Still, right before impact, Lapras forcibly twisted its body, turning its back shell toward Volcarona to absorb the brunt of the hit.

As Bug Buzz blasted into Lapras, the Pokémon cried out in pain, its body spiraling downward.

Boom!

Lapras crashed heavily onto the battlefield.

"Finish it with Giga Drain!" Dylan ordered decisively.

Volcarona descended, its body glowing with deep green energy as particles of life force began drifting toward it from Lapras.

"Lapras! Hydro Pump!"

Suddenly, Red's voice rang out.

"Hm?"

Dylan was momentarily surprised.

Before his eyes, Lapras abruptly lifted its head, water energy surging in its mouth.

It had just been struck by Bug Buzz and slammed into the ground—how could it counterattack so quickly? But the reality was undeniable.

Was it because Lapras had used its shell to tank the damage? Had Red planned all of this?

Dylan narrowed his eyes.

No—if Giga Drain didn't take effect quickly enough, Volcarona would be the one to go down first.

"Bug Buzz!!"

Lapras unleashed a massive torrent of water, while Volcarona abandoned Giga Drain and immediately flapped its wings, sending out another devastating Bug Buzz.

BOOM!

Two explosive sounds erupted almost simultaneously.

Before Hydro Pump even fully connected, Bug Buzz had already struck Volcarona—but the immense water pressure sent Volcarona flying backward in a spray of water.

At the same time, Bug Buzz detonated against Lapras, creating a massive shockwave.

"Holy crap! What a turn of events! I thought Lapras was done for!"

"Wait, I don't get it—how did Lapras fire off Hydro Pump so fast after getting hit and crashing?"

"Volcarona might not withstand that Hydro Pump... Whoa! This challenger is insane! He might actually be the first to take down Dylan's Pokémon!"

"666! This guy is incredible!"

The spectators, who had gathered to watch, erupted into excited discussions.

On the Battlefield

Volcarona struggled to lift itself, but the super-effective Hydro Pump had dealt a serious blow. Combined with the Ancient Power from earlier—quadruple effective against it—Volcarona ultimately couldn't get back up.

It collapsed, completely unconscious.

On the other side, as the dust settled, Lapras was revealed with spiral eyes—it hadn't been able to withstand Bug Buzz either.

"Volcarona and Lapras are both unable to battle!"

Leaf announced the result.

Dylan recalled Volcarona, looking down at its Poké Ball with a smile.

"Not bad at all, Red. You're even stronger than I expected."

He looked up at Red, his expression genuinely impressed.

"..."

Red tugged his cap down slightly.

Even though he had taken down Volcarona, he didn't feel like he had gained any real advantage. In fact, this full-team battle was shaping up to be even tougher than he had anticipated.

Dylan's strategy revolved around an all-Fire-type team, and yet Lapras—a Water-type Pokémon—had already been taken out early on…

That was definitely not a good sign. Especially considering the battle rules had actually been slightly in Red's favor.

"But the battle is just getting started, Red. Your next opponent will be far harder to deal with than Volcarona."

Dylan pulled out another Poké Ball, a smirk forming on his lips.

Red pressed the brim of his cap down further, the shadow obscuring his red eyes and the serious expression on his face.

Harder to deal with than Volcarona?

Did that mean Volcarona was actually the weakest of Dylan's core team?

Red furrowed his brows but quickly relaxed them again.

The stronger Dylan was, the more intense the battle—and the more excited Red became.

Still… how had Blue managed to defeat Dylan and earn the Rota Badge?

Could it be that he had simply lucked out with a favorable type matchup, and Dylan had used weaker Pokémon?

Yeah, that must be it. Red was sure of it.

---

---

---

# 225: Chapter 226: Feel Its Power!

"Incineroar!"

Dylan flipped his hand and tossed out the Poké Ball.

The fierce Incineroar reappeared in front of Dylan, letting out a low growl in a show of intimidation toward Red.

Red: "…"

Seeing Incineroar, Red fell silent.

"Blastoise."

Despite his silence, Red still opened his Poké Ball, and Blastoise landed in front of him once more.

Then came the familiar combination—Intimidate + Fake Out + Parting Shot—a three-hit debuff sequence. Red frowned but found himself helpless against it.

This was truly… an utterly unreasonable weakening strategy.

Next, Dylan switched to Ninetales, bringing the harsh sunlight back into play with its Drought ability.

"Baton Pass." Dylan quickly recalled Ninetales.

"Shell Smash!"

Red hesitated for a moment before issuing his command to Blastoise. With all of Dylan's tactical maneuvers, he couldn't just sit there and watch.

But attacking was pointless—before any move could reach Ninetales, it would likely already be back in its Poké Ball.

Red also refrained from using Rain Dance to counter the harsh sunlight, because Ninetales and Incineroar could rotate seamlessly!

If he used Rain Dance now, Dylan could simply switch back to Incineroar and use Parting Shot to cycle Ninetales in again—it would be meaningless.

Blastoise let out a low growl as a brilliant white light continuously flashed around its body.

Shell Smash!

A Normal-type move that lowers the user's Defense and Special Defense by one stage but drastically increases Attack, Special Attack, and Speed!

It was a high-risk, high-reward setup move—but in this case?

Due to Incineroar's debuffs, Blastoise's Attack had only returned to its normal level, its Special Attack had only risen by one stage, and the only true gain was in Speed.

"Iron Moth! Get ready for battle!"

Dylan tossed out his fourth Pokémon.

With a flash of white light, a Pokémon resembling Volcarona hovered silently in front of him.

It shared many physical traits with Volcarona, but its mechanical design was unmistakable—even its wings looked like they were made of cold, lifeless metal, giving it an eerie presence.

This was Iron Moth!

Beep—Iron Moth, Fire/Poison-type, Paradox Pokémon.

Ability: Quark Drive

Moves: Bug Buzz, Overheat, Morning Sun, Fiery Dance, Sludge Wave, Discharge, Fire Blast, Electric Terrain…

Description: This Pokémon bears a strong resemblance to unidentified flying objects (UFOs) rumored to observe humans, as reported in supernatural magazines.

Dylan's Rotom Pokédex provided detailed information on Iron Moth, but the Pokédexes of Blue and the others failed to recognize it.

"What is that Pokémon?"

Red tried to recall any knowledge of this Pokémon but found nothing in his memory.

From its appearance, could it be a different form of Volcarona?

"Iron Moth is a Paradox Pokémon with Fire and Poison typing," Dylan explained. He never liked keeping type advantages a secret.

"…"

Red's crimson eyes flickered slightly. He was already familiar with Paradox Pokémon—after all, Dylan had gifted him a powerful Roaring Moon!

And with a Fire + Poison typing, that meant Iron Moth had a quadruple weakness to Ground moves.

However, since Iron Moth was airborne, hitting it with a Ground-type move would be a challenge.

Red remained cautious. Based on what Dylan had said earlier, this Iron Moth was likely just as strong as the Volcarona from before—if not even stronger!

"Blastoise, use Hydro Pump!"

Pulling down his cap slightly, Red gave the order—he needed to gauge Iron Moth's strength first.

Blastoise let out a roar as the twin cannons on its back began to glow blue.

Hydro Pump!

Although Blastoise wasn't at full health—having taken a hit from Volcarona's Fiery Dance earlier and being struck twice by Incineroar's Fake Out—

thanks to Shell Smash, it had negated the effects of Parting Shot and boosted its Special Attack by one stage.

As a result, this Hydro Pump was just as strong as the one used against Volcarona earlier!

A massive surge of water erupted, crashing toward Iron Moth like an unstoppable tidal wave.

"Fire Blast." Dylan smiled.

Iron Moth slid backward with an eerie grace, its body glowing red as flames condensed in front of it.

A massive, kanji-shaped blaze erupted forward, colliding directly with the incoming Hydro Pump.

Another head-on clash?! Dylan really had a fiery personality!

Many spectators in the stands were astonished—few trainers would dare to pit a Fire-type move directly against a Water-type move in sheer force.

BOOM!

Fire Blast and Hydro Pump clashed violently, their immense power surging against one another, neither side yielding.

But soon, Blue and the others noticed with shock—Iron Moth's Fire Blast was beginning to overpower Hydro Pump!

"What…?"

The Bird Keeper was utterly stunned. He wasn't mistaken—Fire Blast was actually pushing Hydro Pump back toward Blastoise!

This completely defied logic!

Red narrowed his eyes at the sight.

Blastoise's Hydro Pump wasn't any weaker than before—this wasn't a matter of Blastoise getting weaker.

It was that Iron Moth was even stronger than Volcarona!

"Red, my Iron Moth has a much higher Special Attack than Volcarona!" Dylan grinned.

Indeed, Iron Moth's Special Attack was significantly higher than Volcarona's!

Although the harsh sunlight didn't activate Iron Moth's ability, it still boosted the power of Fire-type moves.

"Blastoise!" Red called out.

Blastoise roared, squeezing out every bit of its strength to intensify Hydro Pump.

"Iron Moth, overpower it!" Dylan commanded.

Iron Moth's mechanical core glowed ominously as Fire Blast surged in size, its advance accelerating.

BOOM!

Hydro Pump was completely overwhelmed and evaporated—Fire Blast crashed directly into Blastoise!

Fiery explosions erupted, sending red-hot embers flying as shockwaves spread outward, drowning out Blastoise's cries of pain.

A searing heatwave rolled across the battlefield, forcing Red to shield his face to avoid taking the brunt of it.

What a powerful Fire Blast!

Red frowned—despite the sunlight's boost and debuffs, the fact that Iron Moth could outright overpower Blastoise's Hydro Pump was ridiculous.

Smoke and dust filled the air, obscuring Blastoise's figure completely.

"Discharge!" Dylan ordered without hesitation.

He didn't need to see Blastoise.

Iron Moth hovered above the battlefield, sparks of electricity crackling around its metallic wings.

A second later, a massive wave of electric energy surged downward—Discharge!

"Protect!" Red commanded immediately.

Electricity was super-effective against Blastoise—he couldn't afford to take the hit.

Within the smoke, Blastoise barely managed to conjure a protective barrier. Discharge struck the shield, golden lightning crackling violently, but it failed to break through.

"Keep using Discharge."

How long could it keep blocking?

Iron Moth's body sparked with energy again as another wave of lightning rained down.

Red knew he had to act fast—Blastoise couldn't stay on the defensive forever.

The second Discharge struck, but Blastoise was gone—a dark hole was left in its place.

"Dig?"

Dylan raised an eyebrow—Iron Moth had no way to counter Dig.

"Prepare Fiery Dance."

Iron Moth's metallic wings shimmered as flames began to dance along them.

At that moment, Blastoise burst from the ground behind it, cloaked in Ground-type energy, lunging toward Iron Moth!

"Fiery Dance!"

Iron Moth swiftly turned, releasing a surge of scorching flames just as Blastoise reached striking range.

BOOM!

Fiery Dance Clashes with Blastoise's Dig!

Blastoise pushed forward with all its might, trying to break through the wall of flames from Iron Moth's Fiery Dance. Red's crimson eyes flickered with sharp focus.

If Dig could land a hit on Iron Moth, even if it meant a trade of damage, it would still be worth it. But landing a hit on Iron Moth was easier said than done.

With a sudden flap of Iron Moth's wings, the flames of Fiery Dance surged violently.

BOOM!

A deafening explosion rocked the battlefield, and the sky was painted in dazzling flames, reflecting in the eyes of every spectator.

"No way—Dig can't get close to Iron Moth! Its Fire-type attacks are way too strong!"

The Psychic-type trainer wiped the sweat from his face, staring in awe at the sea of crimson flames that had engulfed the battlefield.

Even Hydro Pump had failed to overpower Iron Moth's flames—there was no way Dig could withstand the full force of Fiery Dance head-on.

"That's right. Shell Smash did boost Blastoise's Attack, but because of Incineroar's debuffs, it only balanced out to normal levels."

Leaf fanned herself with a poster. "Damn, it's getting hotter in here!"The Rotan Gym's ceiling had already been opened, but with Sunny Day still active and Dylan's Fire-type Pokémon continuously using Fire moves, the temperature remained high.

Yet, not a single spectator left—a little heat was nothing compared to the excitement of witnessing this incredible gym battle!

"There's still some time left on Sunny Day. Blastoise definitely can't match up to Iron Moth. But—wait! That's a Substitute?!"

The Psychic trainer had been analyzing the fight but suddenly widened his eyes in shock.

On the battlefield, Fiery Dance had detonated.

Blastoise should have been knocked down from the sky—yet, in that instant, its body vanished in a puff of white smoke!

That was the effect of a Substitute being destroyed!

Sure enough, the real Blastoise appeared right where its Substitute had been, cloaked in swirling, surging water, charging straight at Iron Moth!

Aqua Jet!

Not only that, but a bright blue energy aura surrounded Blastoise—its Torrent ability had been activated!

"Incredible—he used Substitute underground?" Dylan raised an eyebrow in surprise. But then, a grin slowly spread across his face.

That's just like you, Red!

BANG!

Blastoise's Aqua Jet crashed into Iron Moth before it could retreat, sending the mechanical-winged Pokémon hurtling down like a missile. It slammed hard into the battlefield floor.

Water-type moves were super-effective against Iron Moth, and with Torrent active, Aqua Jet's power was further boosted.Even under Sunny Day, which weakened Water moves, this attack still dealt massive damage to Iron Moth.

On top of that, just like Volcarona, Iron Moth's physical defense was low, making the impact even more devastating.

"Wow! That was incredible! That hit did serious damage to Iron Moth!"

Mitsuki gasped in amazement. This Red guy was insanely skilled.

"Yeah, using Substitute underground—that was a brilliant strategy. He must have known from the start that Dig wouldn't work against Iron Moth."

Leaf was just as impressed. This kind of battle IQ was on another level!

With Iron Moth sent crashing down, Blastoise landed heavily as well—but the moment its feet touched the ground, it winced in pain.

Wave Crash was powerful—but it had recoil damage.

"Discharge."

At that moment, a golden bolt of electricity shot out, striking Blastoise the instant its recoil ended.

ZZZTT!

Sparks crackled violently across Blastoise's body, sending jolts of pain through it as it curled up slightly, letting out a low growl of agony.

Not far away, Iron Moth slowly lifted itself back into the air. Its mechanical wings flickered with residual electricity, its form battered yet still standing.

Its eerie cross-shaped eyes locked onto Blastoise with a cold, mechanical intensity.

"So fast…?" Red's brows furrowed. It was because of Wave Crash's recoil.

If there had been no recoil stun, Blastoise could have chased after Iron Moth—but now, it was too late.

BOOM!

Another Discharge erupted, lightning surging through the battlefield. The explosion of electrical energy sent shockwaves outward.

As the smoke cleared, Blastoise lay unconscious on the ground.

"Blastoise is unable to battle. Iron Moth wins!" Leaf announced the result.

Red silently recalled Blastoise.

That made two of his Pokémon down.

And both were Water-types…

The battle had just gotten even harder.

However, there was some good news—Iron Moth had taken a serious hit, and Sunny Day was about to end.

"Alright, Red—let's see how you plan to handle my Iron Moth next."

Dylan grinned, looking at Red with excitement in his eyes.

This was why he loved battling strong opponents!The thrill of a truly competitive fight, the unexpected twists—this was on a whole different level than facing weak trainers!

Of course, Dylan also loved crushing opponents mercilessly and basking in the admiration and shock of onlookers.

Yeah… alternating between these two feelings was pretty great!

"…"

Red could see the burning anticipation in Dylan's gaze.

His red eyes gleamed slightly as he pulled out an Ultra Ball.

Normally, most of his main Pokémon were caught using standard Poké Balls.

Except for one.

Because… this was a Pokémon that Dylan had personally given to him.

"Dylan—this is the Pokémon you gave me!"

Red threw the Ultra Ball, his crimson eyes flashing with determination.

Now, feel its power!

"Roaring Moon!"

ROOOOAAARRR!

A savage, draconic roar thundered through the Rotan Gym.

(End of Chapter)

# 226: Chapter 227: What’s Dylan’s Final Pokémon?

Roar—!

A wild and ferocious roar echoed through the entire Rota Gym.

A massive, dragon-like Pokémon floated in midair, its savage eyes locked onto Flutter Mane with a menacing glare.

Ancient Roaring Moon!

Its appearance bore some resemblance to Salamence, yet it was distinctively different. The crescent-shaped, blood-red wings, vast and wild, were even larger than its body.

"This is Roaring Moon? Wait… Did big brother Gym Leader give it to him?"

Lillie was surprised. As far as she knew, only her older brother, the Gym Leader, should have a Roaring Moon at this point.

But just now, it seemed that Red had mentioned this was a Pokémon gifted to him by Dylan.

— Beep! Roaring Moon, Dragon/Dark type, the Paradox Pokémon.

— Ability: Protosynthesis.

— Moves: Abyssal Thrust, Dragon Rush, Dragon Dance, Throat Chop, Flamethrower, Zen Headbutt, Scale Shot…

— Description: It could possibly be the very creature documented in the mysterious exploration records under the name Roaring Moon.

What a well-trained Roaring Moon.

Leaf studied the Rotom Pokédex's entry. Compared to her own Flutter Mane, this Roaring Moon that Red had was clearly far more imposing.

"Roaring Moon is certainly powerful, but it has a glaring 4× weakness to Fairy. As long as we find an opening, we can exploit it."

"Still, I think I prefer Flutter Mane over Roaring Moon."

Selene blinked her beautiful eyes. As Dylan's apprentice and employee, the girls had a fair understanding of Paradox Pokémon.

Aside from the six unique ones that belonged to Wave Surge Water, the strongest among the Paradox Pokémon were undeniably Roaring Moon and Flutter Mane, their strength on par with pseudo-legendaries.

But between the two, Selene had a clear favorite—she adored the cold and stylish Flutter Mane. The image of it wielding its crimson energy blades was just way too cool for her tastes!

Absolutely badass!

—

On the Battlefield

The lingering sunlight from Harsh Sunlight continued to bathe the arena, casting a faint red glow over Roaring Moon's body.

Protosynthesis activated!

Roaring Moon's aura became even more ferocious and overwhelming. Under the effects of Protosynthesis, its Attack power increased!

"You've raised it well. Giving it to you was definitely the right choice."

Dylan observed Roaring Moon with interest before nodding in satisfaction.

"It looks like you've made it one of your core Pokémon, Red."

"Roaring Moon is strong."

Red adjusted his cap slightly. He wasn't a Trainer who cared solely about a Pokémon's inherent strength—otherwise, he wouldn't have put so much effort into raising Pikachu.

In reality, Pikachu wasn't that powerful.

But even Red had to admit that Roaring Moon was a monster. With his meticulous training, its power had grown at an astonishing rate.

In terms of raw strength, Roaring Moon was already approaching the level of his ace, Charizard. Whether in terms of innate talent or base stats, Roaring Moon was undeniably exceptional.

Since Dylan had given him this Pokémon, Red had dedicated himself to raising it—not just out of gratitude but because it was now part of his team.

"No need to thank me, Red. I already have my own main Roaring Moon, so rather than keeping this one, I figured it should shine under your command."

Dylan smiled. He could tell Red was about to say something appreciative, but he didn't need to hear it.

Red was someone he recognized as a friend, and besides, he still owed Red a favor.

Red remained silent.

"Alright then, show me how strong your Roaring Moon has become." Dylan smirked.

Though he was using a Fire-type team for this battle, Roaring Moon still benefited from Harsh Sunlight due to its ability. That was going to be a problem.

Glancing at the sunlit battlefield, Dylan noted that Harsh Sunlight would soon fade.

"Roaring Moon, Scale Shot!"

Red immediately focused, his crimson eyes gleaming as he gave his command.

Roaring Moon let out a fierce roar, its two massive wings slicing through the air. A barrage of glowing azure scales shot forth like bullets.

A Dragon-type Scale Shot!

"Fiery Dance!"

Iron Moth's metallic wings shimmered with embers, the motion of its flapping releasing a wave of flames to counter the incoming Scale Shot.

Flames and scales clashed violently, culminating in a sudden explosion.

But before the smoke could fully clear—

A massive shadow loomed overhead!

"Dragon Rush!"

Roaring Moon let out an earth-shaking roar. Pure Dragon energy surged through its body, transforming it into a devastating force of destruction. It tore through the flames, surging forward with overwhelming killing intent.

"Fire Blast!"

Dylan raised an eyebrow—this battle was getting intense.

Iron Moth swiftly flapped backward, releasing a massive, '大'-shaped burst of scorching flames to intercept the incoming Dragon Rush.

BOOM!

Flames and dragon collided, light and shadows intertwining. The spectral dragon form of Dragon Rush roared silently, pressing against Fire Blast with relentless force.

It was clear—Fire Blast couldn't fully hold off the attack!

In the next instant, Fire Blast exploded, its remnants dispersing into a shockwave. But to everyone's shock—

Roaring Moon charged straight through the explosion!

BANG!

Dragon Rush slammed into Iron Moth before it could retreat, sending it crashing to the ground with a painful screech.

"That dragon is insane!"

"This thing is a beast! Roaring Moon? I've never even heard of this Pokémon!"

A Bird Keeper was stunned. Even Blastoise's Hydro Pump couldn't overpower Iron Moth's Fire Blast—yet Roaring Moon had crushed it!

"Unbelievable! This kid is even stronger than that Blue guy from before!"

An older female Trainer sighed in admiration. Comparing oneself to others was just infuriating.

She had thought that the last challenger, Blue, was already an unbeatable prodigy among his generation. But now, here was another one—possibly even stronger!

Is this kid even human?!

—

Back on the Battlefield

Iron Moth lay motionless on the ground, pain coursing through its body as it struggled to rise.

Dragon-type moves weren't super effective against it, but Roaring Moon's Attack stat was simply too high—and Iron Moth's physical defense was quite low.

"Scale Shot!"

Roaring Moon flapped its mighty wings, releasing another storm of azure scales, bombarding Iron Moth and creating a chain of explosions.

A thick cloud of dust billowed into the air. As it cleared, Iron Moth was revealed—collapsed on the ground.

"Iron Moth is unable to battle. Roaring Moon wins."

Leaf announced the result.

"That was a strong Roaring Moon."

Dylan's usual gentle smile remained unchanged as he acknowledged its power.

This Roaring Moon was indeed a force to be reckoned with—especially under Harsh Sunlight.

Before dealing with Roaring Moon, he couldn't afford to set up Sunny Day again.

Otherwise, the boost it provided was just too overwhelming.

Red didn't say anything, but a faint smile appeared on his face.

Harsh Sunlight had now faded, and Protosynthesis had deactivated.

"But what about this?" Dylan tossed out his next Poké Ball.

"Incineroar, enter the battle!"

Red immediately frowned.

Not this damn thing again!

After a brief moment of thought, Red recalled Roaring Moon.

If Roaring Moon stayed in, it would get hit with Intimidate and suffer an Attack drop. That would cripple its offensive power.

Instead, Red tossed out Pikachu's Poké Ball.

Let Pikachu take the 'triple debuff package' from Incineroar.

"So you finally stopped being stubborn, huh?" Dylan chuckled.

No further words were needed. The usual triple Intimidate cycle played out, and then—

Dylan recalled Incineroar.

This time, instead of sending out Ninetales to restart Harsh Sunlight, Dylan reached for his fifth Poké Ball.

"Entei! Enter the battle!"

A flash of white light—

A thunderous, regal roar shook the battlefield as Entei landed with an imposing presence before Dylan.

One of Ho-Oh's legendary guardians—

ENTEI!

"Whoa—! It's Entei!"

The audience erupted into excited exclamations.

Many people in Kanto were well aware that the Rota Gym Leader, Dylan, had captured several Legendary Pokémon.

After all, his "Golden Legend" team was no longer a secret. However, beyond that famous team, other types of Pokémon teams he used also occasionally included Legendary Pokémon.

Of course, for challengers to face a Legendary Pokémon, they needed to be strong enough—and it also depended on which team Dylan chose to use for the battle.

But still…

"Entei is only his fifth Pokémon?"

A Psychic-type Trainer watched the majestic and awe-inspiring Entei, curious about what Dylan's final Fire-type Pokémon would be.

As for Ninetales and Incineroar, their roles in the team were clearly support-based. However, while Ninetales was mostly a setup Pokémon, Incineroar was an entirely different story—it was actually very strong.

Dylan's apprentice, Selene, had already proven this, and Incineroar was one of Dylan's veteran Pokémon—a reliable old guard. While it served as a support Pokémon in this battle, it was by no means weak.

In fact, no one had ever pushed Dylan to the point where he needed to rely on Incineroar for offense.

So by that logic, Entei was Dylan's third battle-focused Pokémon.

But what about the final one?

Dylan had a habit—the later he sent out a Pokémon, the harder it was to deal with!

This time, though, there was finally a chance to see it!

"This kid might actually force Dylan to send out his sixth Pokémon!" A Bird Keeper marveled. Red still had four Pokémon left!

Entei was powerful, but who said that a Legendary Pokémon was unbeatable?

"Could it be Gouging Fire? Or maybe Reshiram?" Selene and the others speculated eagerly.

"It's not Gouging Fire—I saw earlier that its Poké Ball wasn't added to the team. And as for the White Dragon… Big Brother wouldn't use Reshiram in a Gym Battle that doesn't involve the Legendary Badge."

Lillie shook her head. Entei was a recent capture, and while powerful, it still wasn't as strong as Gouging Fire.

But Lillie was certain—Gouging Fire wasn't in this battle.

"Then it must be that one."

Leaf and the others immediately guessed which Fire-type Pokémon Dylan was saving as his final trump card.

After all, Dylan had always been fond of that Pokémon—and ever since acquiring it, he had spent a great deal of time raising it.

Moreover…

Considering the recent rumors, Lillie realized that now was the perfect time for Dylan to finally use that Pokémon in battle.

"Entei…!"

Red's crimson eyes blazed with excitement as he stared at the regal and imposing Entei.

A Legendary Pokémon—Entei!

This kind of opponent—!

"Well then, Red~"

Dylan smiled, extending his hand as he spoke.

"How will you handle my Entei?"

"Come back, Pikachu."

Without hesitation, Red recalled his weakened Pikachu and threw Roaring Moon's Poké Ball back onto the field.

The ancient, savage dragon loomed over Entei, its piercing gaze locked onto the Legendary Beast.

But Entei stood firm, unshaken, its majestic, commanding eyes meeting Roaring Moon's challenge head-on.

Red lowered his cap, his eyes burning with battle spirit.

He could even feel his own heartbeat pounding with exhilaration.

This battle… felt amazing!

Defeating a Legendary Pokémon—

That achievement would be his—

TODAY!

# 227: Chapter 228: Facing a Legend!

Face Entei! Face a Legend!

For Red, this was both a tough challenge and an exciting opportunity—exactly the kind of battle he loved!

Only in fights like these could he truly feel his own existence and his purpose as a Trainer.

He craved battle. He dreamed of battle. He lived for battle.

Victory and honor? Those were just bonuses that came along the way.

Red knew very well—he was fundamentally different from Blue.

Blue chased the peak of the Trainer world because he wanted to be the Champion, to be recognized as the strongest Trainer in the world.

That had been Blue's dream since childhood.

But Red?

He sought strength for a different reason—to battle stronger opponents.

As long as he kept getting stronger, the opponents he encountered would also be stronger.

He challenged the Indigo League because the Kanto Elite Four were the strongest Trainers in the region.

He came to Rota Gym because its Gym Leader was Dylan!

"Scale Shot!"

Red's crimson eyes burned with passion, his voice brimming with excitement and intensity—a side of him rarely seen.

Roaring Moon's blood-red wings sliced through the air, releasing a rain of glowing blue scales that shot down like bullets.

A Dragon-type Scale Shot!

"Extreme Speed."

Dylan chuckled.

Entei lowered its stance, its muscular legs tensing.

A brilliant white light flared behind it—then boom!

In an instant, Entei shot forward like a lightning bolt!

A Normal-type priority move—Extreme Speed.

Scale Shot covered a wide area, but Extreme Speed was simply too fast—before the scales could land, Entei had already vanished!

"Roaring Moon, fly higher!"

Red's sharp gaze locked onto the orange blur as Entei dashed across the battlefield at insane speed.

Roaring Moon flapped its massive wings, soaring higher into the air.

But suddenly—

A fiery blur leaped up, heading straight for Roaring Moon!

It was Entei!

Boosted by Extreme Speed, it moved faster than expected, even catching Roaring Moon off guard!

BANG!

Entei's tackle slammed into Roaring Moon's midsection!

The dragon gritted its teeth, letting out a pained growl.

Roaring Moon was a brutal physical attacker—aside from Special Attack and Defense, it had no weaknesses.

With one of the highest Attack stats among non-Legendary Pokémon, blazing speed, and flight capabilities, it was an absolute powerhouse.

Its low Defense was its only true flaw.

"Rock Slide!"

Red didn't hesitate.

Even with its low Defense, Roaring Moon wasn't going to lose to a simple Extreme Speed!

With a fierce roar, Roaring Moon summoned massive boulders, launching them straight at Entei.

"Extreme Speed."

Once again, Entei vanished.

Using the momentum from its own speed, it sidestepped the incoming boulders, dodging them with ease!

"Don't let it escape! Dragon Rush!"

Red immediately pressed the attack.

Extreme Speed was a frustrating move to deal with, but now that Entei was airborne, it had no way to regain its momentum!

This was the perfect chance to strike!

ROAR!

Roaring Moon's wings flared wide, its entire body radiating pure Dragon energy.

The energy formed into the shape of a fearsome dragon, surrounding Roaring Moon's body.

Dragon Rush!

The feral dragon dived down, slamming toward Entei with unstoppable force!

"Sacred Fire!"

Dylan reacted instantly.

Entei let out a thunderous roar, its body erupting in violet flames!

Sacred Fire—a powerful Fire-type move originally exclusive to Ho-Oh, but Entei could also use it.

However, Entei's Sacred Fire was different—

Unlike Ho-Oh's radiant multicolored flames, Entei's version burned with deep violet hues.

Dragon Rush and Sacred Fire collided—

BOOM!

The impact sent waves of energy rippling through the battlefield.

The temperature inside the Gym rapidly soared from the heat of battle.

And then—

BANG!

Sacred Fire exploded, sending out a wave of scorching flames.

But before the flames could completely disperse—

A massive shadow broke through the firestorm!

Roaring Moon forced its way through the explosion!

CRASH!

Dragon Rush slammed into Entei!

The Legendary Beast roared in pain, but Roaring Moon didn't stop.

With unstoppable momentum, Roaring Moon slammed Entei straight into the ground!

BOOM!

The battlefield trembled from the sheer force of impact.

The ground beneath Entei cracked apart, leaving deep fissures.

Dust and debris filled the air.

"Damn! That hit was brutal!"

"This Roaring Moon is crazy strong!"

"No way—it just bulldozed through the explosion and kept going?! That's insane!"

"It must have high Special Defense… Dragon-type resists Fire, so Entei's attacks aren't as effective."

The crowd buzzed with excitement, as more and more spectators gathered to watch the battle unfold.

Word had already spread through Rota—

Dylan, the Gym Leader, was going all-out in a full-team battle against a challenger!

Even casual onlookers from Rota's shopping district had rushed over to see.

Trainers from nearby areas had also arrived, drawn by the intensity of the fight.

Dylan was a rising legend, and many people wanted to see him battle firsthand.

Recently, there were even rumors that a group from Galar had come to Rota to recruit Dylan.

"Roaring Moon! Rock Slide!"

Red ignored the growing audience.

He didn't care about the crowd—he never had.

He was completely focused on the battle.

This was his chance to press his advantage—he wasn't going to waste it!

Roaring Moon soared upward, its wings stirring the air, summoning a hail of massive boulders.

A powerful Rock Slide!

"Extreme Speed!"

Suddenly—

BOOM!

The orange blur shot forward again, slamming into Roaring Moon's chest!

Roaring Moon roared in fury, its expression twisted into a feral snarl.

"Dragon Claw!"

Red didn't panic—he knew Roaring Moon excelled in close combat!

Roaring Moon's claws gleamed with draconic energy, slashing toward Entei.

"Crunch—bite it!"

Entei's jaws snapped open, revealing gleaming black fangs.

BAM!

It caught Roaring Moon's claw in its teeth, biting down with immense force!

"Will-O-Wisp!"

Entei's body flared with eerie blue flames!

The blue fire engulfed Roaring Moon—

BURNED!

Roaring Moon let out a pained roar—its attack power was crippled!

"Red let his guard down! Tch—this burn is a disaster for Roaring Moon."

Leaf clicked her tongue in frustration. Among all status conditions, burns were the most devastating for physical attackers.

Not only did burns cause chip damage over time, but more importantly, they severely reduced a Pokémon's Attack stat! (In the games, Attack is halved when burned.)

For a close-combat physical powerhouse like Roaring Moon, this was practically a death sentence. How was it supposed to fight now?

"Roaring Moon is basically crippled now. What a wasted opportunity…" A Bird Keeper sighed in disappointment.

"It was almost impossible to avoid. Entei was willing to take a hit just to land the burn… And honestly, who would expect a legendary Pokémon like Entei to even use Will-O-Wisp?"

A young woman rolled her eyes. This was Entei!

As a legendary Pokémon, wasn't it supposed to dominate with overwhelming power? That was the image everyone had of legendary Pokémon!

But this Entei was using Will-O-Wisp? Is this really how you use a legendary?!

"This is Dylan we're talking about. Nothing he does is surprising. Besides, Fire-type moves that inflict burns are his specialty." A Psychic-type Trainer shrugged.

Dylan had once been called an "Unorthodox Trainer" for his unconventional battle strategies. An Entei using Will-O-Wisp? That was nothing unusual for him.

On the Battlefield

"Red, looks like you weren't careful enough."

Dylan smirked as he addressed his opponent.

"..."

Red remained silent, staring at his Roaring Moon, which had managed to regain altitude.

Despite its intimidating presence, its offensive threat had plummeted due to the burn.

Roaring Moon's entire battle style revolved around overwhelming physical force. Now that its Attack was cut in half, it couldn't hit hard anymore.

What was it supposed to do now? Start using special attacks instead?

Even with its weakened Attack, Roaring Moon's physical power was still probably higher than its Special Attack.

Roaring Moon wasn't Salamence—it couldn't function as a mixed attacker. It was a pure physical powerhouse.

"Return, Roaring Moon."

Red didn't hesitate and recalled Roaring Moon.

"A smart choice."

Dylan chuckled. Cold and rational, as always.

A burned Roaring Moon stood no chance against Entei. Red had immediately recognized this and switched out, choosing to preserve his Pokémon for later opportunities.

"Snorlax."

Red tossed out his fifth Pokémon. He did not send out Pikachu.

Pikachu relied on speed and agility for fast-paced attacks—but Entei had Extreme Speed, making it a poor matchup.

Since Dylan was using a Fire-type team, Venusaur was also not an option.

A flash of white light revealed Snorlax, landing with a heavy thud in front of Red.

"Much stronger than before."

Ilene glanced at Snorlax's battle data. Compared to a few months ago, Red's Snorlax had made huge progress—in both strength and move versatility.

No wonder even Dylan acknowledged Red as a monster of a Trainer. His growth rate was terrifying.

If it weren't for Dylan, then Red and Blue would unquestionably be the strongest rising stars of Kanto's new generation.

"This Red is incredible… I wonder who would win between him and Blue?"

Lillie was curious. So far, Red seemed stronger than Blue.

But Blue had battled weeks ago, and for Trainers like Red and Blue, who were still in their rapid growth phase, every day of progress was crucial.

"Who knows? We'll find out at the end of the year." Leaf shrugged.

Both Red and Blue were aiming for the Kanto Championship. Their battle was inevitable.

"Snorlax, huh?"

Dylan raised an eyebrow. If he remembered correctly, this Snorlax had the Thick Fat ability.

Thick Fat: Reduces damage taken from Fire- and Ice-type attacks.

That ability gave Snorlax a huge advantage against Fire-types.

Tch—Entei's strongest Fire-type attacks were now useless.

Dylan sighed. Entei could breathe fire, but Roaring Moon resisted Fire, and Snorlax had Thick Fat.

Without Harsh Sunlight to boost its power, Entei's Fire moves wouldn't be effective at all.

"Snorlax, Earthquake!"

Red's command was calm and firm.

Snorlax let out a deep rumble, then slammed its massive arms into the ground.

BOOM!

The battlefield shook violently, sending a wave of seismic force rippling forward.

A Ground-type Earthquake!

"Extreme Speed!"

Entei dashed forward, just as the Earthquake shockwave closed in.

At the last moment, it leaped high into the air, evading the brunt of the attack while racing straight for Snorlax.

BANG!

Extreme Speed struck Snorlax hard!

Snorlax grunted in pain. Its Defense wasn't great, but its massive bulk meant it could take hits exceptionally well.

Extreme Speed hurt, but not enough to be a serious threat.

"Rock Slide!" Red immediately countered.

Large boulders materialized and came crashing down toward Entei.

But—

The white streak of light didn't fade.

The moment Entei landed, it instantly launched into motion again.

Extreme Speed!

With fluid, lightning-fast movements, Entei dodged between the falling boulders, maneuvering effortlessly through the barrage.

Then—BOOM!

Another Extreme Speed slammed into Snorlax.

Right in the face!

Snorlax roared in frustration, shaking off the hit as it swung its huge arms at Entei.

But Entei had already leaped back, dodging effortlessly.

Snorlax growled angrily—it really wanted to catch this annoying four-legged dog, but no matter how hard it tried, it simply wasn't fast enough.

It wanted to fight head-on, but its opponent refused to play fair.

Frustration. jpg

"Come on, fight me properly!"

"This won't work. Extreme Speed is too fast—we need a different approach."

Red's eyes narrowed.

Entei's Extreme Speed was way too polished. Snorlax was strong, but its slower movements meant that it could never catch Entei in a direct confrontation.

Even though Extreme Speed's individual damage wasn't high, Entei could keep spamming it to chip away at Snorlax's health while staying untouched.

"Man, Extreme Speed is so broken!"

Selene couldn't help but admire the move.

"Look at Snorlax—it looks completely helpless against Entei!"

On the battlefield, Snorlax looked like a clumsy oaf, while Entei darted around gracefully, striking and retreating with perfect precision.

Snorlax was getting outplayed so hard, it was almost embarrassing.

"This bear is getting humiliated!"

Third update for the day!
